The masked man’s eyes reddened with humiliation.

Behind him, Geom Mugeuk had already arrived. The problem was that these two were not the only ones to appear here.

“Sixth Wolf!”

At the sound of someone calling him, the masked man’s heart sank.

Bi Sain and the rest of the Thirteen Wolves of the Sado Alliance revealed themselves.

The masked man was the sixth of the Thirteen Wolves of the Sado Alliance—Sixth Wolf. Their leader, First Wolf Baek Cheolgi, was truly furious.

“Was it really you who betrayed us?”

Even seeing it with his own eyes, Baek Cheolgi could not believe it. If someone had asked him to choose, among the thirteen Thirteen Wolves of the Sado Alliance, the ones least likely to betray them, Sixth Wolf would have been the first person he chose.

“Why in the world did you do this? Did you not know that the Thirteen Wolves of the Sado Alliance must not involve themselves in politics?”

Sixth Wolf removed his mask.

“Why not?”

“What?”

“Why must we live bound by some rule when we don’t even know who made it?”

“You bastard. Then what more did you want, living as a martial artist? If you hated living this way, you should never have sworn loyalty in the first place!”

“Because I didn’t know. Because I didn’t know that the martial arts I honed my entire life would lead to such a pathetic life! Because I didn’t know my life would become one where I wandered around in a pack just to protect the Eldest Young Master!”

At that moment, Bi Sain’s expression hardened. He had not known Sixth Wolf had followed him with such feelings. Did the others feel the same?

First Wolf shouted at him.

“Enough excuses! What did that side promise you? Just what did they offer to let you escape this pathetic life?”

Sixth Wolf could not answer. It was not something he could say to First Wolf or to his other comrades. He could not bring himself to say that he had been promised an enormous sum of money and a high position.

“I am not a traitor. I was only trying to find my own life!”

Shouting fiercely, Sixth Wolf chose the person he would take down with him. The person he chose was Geom Mugeuk.

“This is all because of you!”

Sixth Wolf threw himself toward Geom Mugeuk.

Even when he had not been agitated, he had been no match for him, and now he was wounded as well.

Just as he unleashed his techniques at Geom Mugeuk—

Thuk!

A sword blade pierced through his back and emerged from his chest.

As Sixth Wolf slowly turned his head, Bi Sain was standing behind him.

“……This is all because of you.”

At Sixth Wolf’s resentful words, Bi Sain said bluntly,

“A moment ago you said it was because of the Second Young Master.”

As if he had no desire to continue the conversation, Bi Sain drew out his sword.

Beneath the moonlight, a fountain of blood sprayed from Sixth Wolf’s body. Had Geom Mugeuk remained still, he would have been drenched in blood, but he stepped aside. After all, this fight was a family quarrel within the Sado Alliance.

Bi Sain, holding the sword from which blood dripped steadily, asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Now, tell me. Who is the mastermind?”

Chapter 112: Can You Swim?
--------------------------
Bi Sain was furious.

I knew where that fury came from.

It was probably because of what Sixth Wolf had said earlier.

A life spent flocking around just to protect the First Young Master.

Of course it would have offended him, and he must have felt looked down upon as well.

If it had been only a matter of Sixth Wolf, he might have been able to forget it by killing him.

But this was the problem: the suspicion that the other Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao might think the same way. That suspicion would torment him for a very long time.

Of course, I had no intention of soothing him. Fundamentally, he was my enemy, and I also intended to use their division and Bi Sain’s confused state of mind.

Then First Wolf stepped forward.

“Should we not have kept Sixth Wolf alive and used him as a witness?”

His voice was slightly flushed. He, too, could not help being flustered and agitated in the face of a comrade’s betrayal and death.

Bi Sain gave no answer. I could guess why. Right now, First Wolf’s words must have sounded to him like this.

—He was like a brother to me. How could you kill him like that?

Bi Sain’s gaze turned toward the remaining Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao standing behind First Wolf. They stood there with expressions no different from usual.

But at this moment, Bi Sain would find them unfamiliar.

That was when I stepped in.

“Are you not the witnesses? Even excluding Young Master Bi, are there not six witnesses who saw Sixth Wolf betray you?”

This was what First Wolf was worried about.

“If they accuse all of us of being in league with him, they may not believe us.”

“Are you saying that the testimony of six of the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao, who represent the Evil Dao Alliance, will not hold? If so, do you people have any meaning in living as warriors of the Evil Dao Alliance?”

First Wolf and the rest of the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao said nothing. If one thought about it, it must truly feel wretched.

Then Bi Sain, who had been listening in silence, suddenly shouted.

“Enough of this useless talk!”

It was only natural that he reacted so irritably.

“Who is behind this?”

“I cannot tell you here.”

I poured oil onto the blazing fire.

“Do you really think there is only one traitor?”

The Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao all sent cold gazes at me at once.

This reaction was actually the one Bi Sain should have shown first. But Bi Sain was the slowest to react. It was because he was having the same suspicion.

“Would it not be best to be careful until everything is made clear? Let us discuss that matter alone.”

I was driving Bi Sain into a corner while dividing him from the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao. This was something I had to grab Bi Sain by the collar and drag forward. I had no intention of losing the initiative until the very moment the scheme came to fruition.

Before taking a seat alone with Bi Sain, I briefly spoke with the Extreme Evil Soma through sound transmission.

—I intend to keep Bi Sain apart from the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao for the time being.

—You must have something in mind.

—If I am to move Bi Sain the way I want, I need some time alone with him.

In truth, there was another reason, but it was one I could not tell the Extreme Evil Soma.

The quick-witted Extreme Evil Soma suddenly asked.

—Is that truly the only reason?

—No.

When I answered honestly, the Extreme Evil Soma instead smiled with his eyes.

—As expected, Second Young Master was wearing the same mask as I.

—That is correct. No one believed me when I said I was not such a good person, but as expected, Lord Soma recognizes it. Then I will see you later at the safe house near the main headquarters of the Evil Dao Alliance.

Given the nature of the matter, the Extreme Evil Soma accepted my request. At least when it came to public affairs, he handled things more cleanly than anyone else.

* * *

I stood side by side with Bi Sain on a hill east of Baegya Valley.

“Who is behind this?”

“If I tell you, what will you do for me? Surely you were not thinking of receiving such important information for free?”

Bi Sain’s expression stiffened slightly.

“Damn it! Why is it so hard to hear a single name? Tell me like a man!”

“That is not being manly. That is being a fool who accepts a loss for the sake of a moment’s smugness. I am someone who believes that a transaction must always involve an exchange of proper value. Problems in a deal always arise when one side is dissatisfied. Let us try to balance the scales properly.”

“What do you want?”

There was something I wanted from him. After returning to the past, there were fortuitous encounters I had decided to gather whenever the opportunity arose. What I was about to tell him now was one of them.

“Let me enter the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils.”

The Cave of Ten Thousand Evils.

It was the sacred ground of the Evil Dao Alliance, a place only the alliance leader and his successor could enter.

Three hundred years ago, the absolute ruler of the Evil Dao Alliance, the Patriarch of Ten Thousand Evils, had healed his injuries there after a fierce battle with the Heavenly Demon of that time, and the Evil Dao Alliance had made the place their sacred ground.

Bi Sain was startled.

“Why do you want to enter that place?”

“I have wanted to enter it since long ago.”

“Then you must know. No outsider may ever be allowed into the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils.”

“That is why I am asking now, is it not? With your life as collateral.”

“Do not exaggerate. Even without knowing who was behind this, I have survived until now. So do not act as though you possess something so extraordinary.”

“You were alive, but truly in danger. With a traitor even behind your back, no less. If you had not met me, that traitor would still be eating and sleeping together with you, Young Master Bi.”

Perhaps because he could not refute those words at least, Bi Sain was silent for a moment before asking me.

“Do you know what kind of place the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils is?”

The reason he asked like this was because there was nothing particularly special there.

It was merely a place commemorating where the Patriarch of Ten Thousand Evils had healed his injuries, no different from building a pavilion in a scenic spot.

“I heard that three hundred years ago, the Heavenly Demon and the Patriarch of Ten Thousand Evils fought, and that the details of that event were recorded in the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils. As someone who will become the next Heavenly Demon, I wish to read about that battle in detail. Unfortunately, no such records remain in our cult.”

This was Bi Sain’s concern.

“Letting you into that place is not a major problem. However, if this fact is revealed, it could become a major problem.”

“You need not worry about that. I will never reveal that I entered the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils. And suppose I do reveal it. Then you can simply deny it, can you not? If you say it is not true, who would believe my words?”

In the end, he would accept. Compared to what he would gain, the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils was nothing. Though it had been designated sacred ground, it was no more than a sort of memorial hall.

The secret of the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils would be revealed more than ten years from now. It was something that happened after this Bi Sain before my eyes was already dead.

“If you dislike it, forget it. I do not particularly wish to enter badly enough to beg like this. Let us pretend this never happened.”

“Second Young Master, do not be petty.”

“Young Master Bi, you are the one being petty with your own life.”

“What does that mean?”

“It means that even if others may hesitate, you alone must not. There is nothing in this world more important than life. All the more if that life is a precious one that will ascend to the position of Evil Dao Alliance Leader once you get past this crisis. Throw everything else aside and think of just one thing: your life!”

In the end, Bi Sain let out a long breath and accepted the proposal.

“Very well. I will let you enter the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils.”

He was in a situation where, if he could learn who was behind this, he had to learn it no matter the cost.

“Instead, I have two conditions.”

“Speak.”

“First, you must reveal who is behind this beforehand.”

I had expected him to make such a demand.

“I will tell you at the entrance to the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils.”

“Fine.”

For the first condition, we readily accepted each other’s demands.

“Second, you have exactly one double-hour.”

“That is too short. Give me three double-hours.”

“Two double-hours. No more.”

“Fine.”

Bi Sain drew his sword. I also drew the Black Demon Sword and struck blade against blade. Clang! A clear sound rang out as a token of our promise.

“By any chance, is the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils on the way back from here to the main headquarters of the Evil Dao Alliance?”

“It is.”

“Then let the two of us depart immediately.”

Bi Sain was startled by the unexpected proposal.

“Are you saying we should leave the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao behind?”

“That is right. Let us leave the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao behind, and the Extreme Evil Soma as well.”

Bi Sain started to say something, then stopped. He could not bring himself to ask if it would not be dangerous.

Until now, seeing me move alone had wounded his pride. And now he could not possibly bring up the question of danger like a coward.

“Right now, going as a pair will be safest.”

“Why is that?”

“Because you and the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao possess such high martial arts, it is not easy to place surveillance around you. That must be why they recruited Sixth Wolf.”

Bi Sain nodded, agreeing with those words.

“Now that Sixth Wolf is dead, there is a gap in their surveillance. But soon they will learn of Sixth Wolf’s death and send a new expert to watch you. If we are going to leave, it must be right now. Leave for the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils with me now. Leave the Extreme Evil Soma and the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao behind, and let the two of us go. Moving contrary to your opponent’s expectations is the way to defeat them.”

Even so, Bi Sain could not make the decision easily. In the end, he revealed his honest feelings.

“If we die doing that, it will all be for nothing, will it not?”

His conflicted face looked even more distorted. A face unable to convey his feelings properly. Perhaps pity for that was one of the reasons I wanted to spare him.

“Whether having the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao with you will raise your chances of living or your chances of dying is something we cannot know. As I said earlier, can you be certain there is no other spy among the remaining Thirteen Wolves?”

Bi Sain could not answer confidently. There was no guarantee they had not planted spies twofold or threefold. Only First Wolf Baek Cheolgi could be trusted with certainty.

“Besides, if you are to let me into the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils, should the two of us not move alone? Would it be all right if they knew? That you let me into the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils? Not only them, but even the Extreme Evil Soma would learn of it.”

Those words were decisive.

“Fine. Let the two of us go.”

“You have thought well.”

Resolve filled Bi Sain’s face.

Originally, this was not a decision he should have made. He should not have moved according to the other party’s intentions so easily. If the other party had been a villain, he would already have been ruined.

But Bi Sain, this time, you did well.

I do not know how your fate will flow, but joining hands with me may be the only way to change the direction of the wheel of your tragic destiny. Pull the reins harder so that you can change that direction.

“I will help you become the leader of the Evil Dao Alliance.”

“The people of the world will laugh. If I become the leader of the Evil Dao Alliance with your help.”

“The people of the world will never know that fact. Someday, when I become the Heavenly Demon and you become the leader of the Evil Dao Alliance, and we meet, let us recall this moment and shake hands once.”

Bi Sain stared at me as if piercing through me. How could I know a person’s heart? Whether this moment disgusted him so much that he would try to kill me, or whether he would truly be grateful for this day and take my hand.

“Let us go.”

I began running first, and he ran after me.

We left Baegya Valley in an instant.

* * *

More than ten days later, we had run only along deserted roads and at last arrived atop a cliff. Since he knew there had been no surveillance or pursuit at all on the way here, Bi Sain acknowledged that my judgment had been correct.

Shwaaaaaaak.

Looking down at the pouring waterfall, Bi Sain asked.

“Can you swim?”

“I can even eat a meal underwater while dealing with the Changjiang Water Ghosts.”

“Then follow me.”

Bi Sain leapt down beneath the waterfall first, and I jumped after him.

The entrance to the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils was in a place that could never be found unless one knew its location. We swam in through a narrow entrance beneath the waterfall.

It was in a place where the current was strong, so it was difficult to see in the first place, and it was a narrow hole that looked as though one would get stuck and never come out if one entered.

We passed through that entrance and swam along the narrow passage. Only after going so deep that fear seized us that we might drown if we went any farther were we able to emerge from the water.

The waterway was connected to a pond, and that pond was inside a cave.

“They say the Patriarch of Ten Thousand Evils, injured in his battle with the Heavenly Demon, drifted into this place.”

“For that narrow passage to lead here, heaven must have saved him.”

“That is why we made this place our sacred ground.”

We stood side by side before the cave. Originally, it had been a cave that continued straight inward, but they had blocked the entrance and made a door.

“This door can only be opened by infusing inner energy in a set method. If one tries to force it open, this place will collapse. Now, tell me. Who is behind this?”

I immediately revealed the mastermind to him.

“Grand Elder Seok Gwanchu of the Evil Dao Alliance.”

Bi Sain was shocked. It was truly a name he had never expected.

“That cannot be. Elder Seok withdrew long ago and entered the Council of Elders. Do you have proof that it is him?”

“Heuksalju confessed.”

“Where is he now?”

“Dead.”

Bi Sain’s expression stiffened.

“Are you saying Elder Seok is behind this without any proof?”

“Why is there no proof? I, the second son of the Heavenly Demon, am speaking as a witness.”

“!”

“If you do not trust me, then you should not have been standing here in the first place. What exactly are you doing?”

At my rebuke, Bi Sain let out a deep sigh and said.

“No. I trust you. I came because I trust you. It is only that I cannot believe it is Elder Seok.”

That was how much respect Seok Gwanchu received from the warriors of the Evil Dao Alliance.

“Elder Seok is trying to make his grandson the successor.”

Only then did Bi Sain understand the situation.

“Ah! So that was it. Elder Seok was extremely proud of his grandson. He boasted about him several times in front of me. Back then… I thought his affection for his grandson was excessive, but thinking about it now, it seems that was not all there was to it.”

A cold murderous intent flashed through Bi Sain’s eyes. Seok Gwanchu, his grandson, and the various words and events he had casually overlooked until now must be taking on new meaning and freezing his heart cold.

“Now, it is your turn to keep your promise.”

Bi Sain kept his promise like a man. When he placed his palm against the wall and infused his inner energy, the door opened.

“The fact that you entered this place remains a secret until death. And you have exactly two double-hours. If you do not come out by then, I will go in.”

Leaving him behind, I slowly walked into the Cave of Ten Thousand Evils.

Chapter 113: Please, Yeonja.
----------------------------
I slowly walked into Mansa Cave.

Because the Evil Dao Alliance had made it into a holy site, the interior was well maintained. The air circulated well, and thanks to the night pearls embedded here and there, it was bright inside.

After walking in for some time, I came upon a wide space. This was Mansa Cave. On the newly built walls, the life of Patriarch Mansa had been recorded in writing and pictures.

From his birth, to his unfortunate childhood, to the days he devoted himself to arduous martial training, the heroic tales of his youth, and the chronicle of how he later became a great master of the Evil Path—all of it was written there.

But that was all. There was nothing else. This was why Bisain had allowed me inside. After all, it was nothing more than a place where the life story of an Evil Path master, once called a legend three hundred years ago, had been recorded.

The pinnacle of his life was his battle against the Heavenly Demon.

Though he had not won, the fact that he survived that battle was what made him a figure still spoken of to this day.

According to the records written on the wall, he treated his injuries and stayed here for ten days before leaving. It said he was fortunate there was water here, which allowed him to endure.

After that, it was written that he went into seclusion and never appeared in the murim again.

There was no mention of the fact that, before he died, he had left behind a discourse on his martial learning here.

Then how did that become known?

Ten years from now, an earthquake would strike this place, the wall would collapse, and a new space would be discovered.

I drew the Black Demon Sword.

Ziiing.

Sky-blue sword force settled over the Black Demon Sword.

I drove the sword clad in sword force into the lower part of the western wall. Following the boundary line at the bottom of the wall that had been built for the writing and paintings, I cut out a neat opening large enough for one person to pass through.

The wall that had collapsed was precisely this western wall. It was not very thick, so after cutting deep enough to pierce through it, I used my internal energy to push the stone slab inward, and it slid in.

I crawled inside. This inner space was where Patriarch Mansa had left behind his thoughts on his martial learning before his death.

On the wall was written the discourse on martial learning he had left behind. Judging by how he had hidden it separately in a secret space, it seemed he had not wanted someone without the proper fate to see its contents.

I slowly read it.

It was a discourse on martial learning as a whole, based on the enlightenment he had gained through his battle with the Heavenly Demon.

Patriarch Mansa’s martial learning was immensely lofty and profound. To ordinary masters, it was nothing but grasping at clouds, something they could read a hundred times to no avail. But I was no ordinary master.

I read it again and again. It was the principle left behind by the greatest master of the Evil Dao in the past three hundred years. There was no need to memorize it. It belonged to the realm of understanding.

At first, I gained enlightenment from the differences. A new lesson granted by Evil Path martial arts utterly unlike the demonic arts of our cult.

But the more I read it over and over, the more I came to understand that, in the end, all martial arts began from a single root, and I gained enlightenment from their sameness.

They were alike, yet different. Which parts were different, and which parts were the same? Knowing the profundity granted by that subtle difference was what separated an unparalleled master from those who were not.

I drew my sword and swung it, then read the words again, then once more performed forms like a madman. This experience was fresh and intense.

If I had only learned demonic arts, or if I had only learned the martial arts of the Evil Path, then perhaps his teachings would not have struck me so deeply.

But though my foundation lay in demonic arts, I had lived my whole life transcending factions. That was why Patriarch Mansa’s final enlightenment resonated so greatly with me.

Then, at some point while I was performing the forms of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, I realized it.

Before I knew it, I had achieved grand completion of the twelfth stage of the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

Like water naturally flowing downward, Patriarch Mansa’s teachings had led me to the next stage.

Grand completion of the tenth stage and grand completion of the twelfth stage were realms on entirely different levels. One martial art had reached its ultimate realm and finally been perfected.

“Ah!”

I was delighted. Even in my previous life, I had never achieved grand completion of the twelfth stage in any martial art. Now that I had reached grand completion of the twelfth stage in a profound martial art like the Flying Heaven Sword Art, I was so happy I felt as if I might fly.

I prostrated myself on the spot and bowed. Though he had been a great master of the Evil Path, I offered my sincere gratitude.

“Thank you truly for leaving behind such a great teaching. Though a person of the Evil Dao did not receive this precious fate, someone who intends to help your descendant has received it, so please do not be too disappointed.”

Just as I bowed and was about to rise, something caught my eye.

Something was hidden in a place that could only be seen while prostrating oneself. When I went closer and looked, it was a letter. A letter left behind by Patriarch Mansa. Among its contents, the final passage entered my eyes.

“…I realized that even if I consumed this Ten-Thousand-Year Black Lingzhi, I would not be able to kill the Heavenly Demon. Therefore, I do not take it and instead leave it to later generations. May the one fated for it cherish this affinity.”

“Ten-Thousand-Year Black Lingzhi?”

Startled, I searched the area around where the letter had been placed. Behind the letter was a small hole. When I looked inside, a Ten-Thousand-Year Black Lingzhi was truly growing there.

When I infused a little internal energy and pushed, the earthen wall collapsed, revealing a small space.

Astonishingly, what was growing there was indeed a Ten-Thousand-Year Black Lingzhi. It was a spiritual medicine more effective than any elixir I had consumed until now.

Had I not bowed to Patriarch Mansa out of gratitude, I would not have discovered the letter, nor would I have discovered the Ten-Thousand-Year Black Lingzhi. Whether Patriarch Mansa had intended it or whether it was coincidence, I had obtained a second fortuitous encounter.

‘Thank you once again.’

I carefully harvested the Ten-Thousand-Year Black Lingzhi. The Ten-Thousand-Year Black Lingzhi was actually smaller than an ordinary lingzhi. The wondrous energy accumulated over long ages was contained within this small mushroom.

I consumed the Ten-Thousand-Year Black Lingzhi on the spot.

An immense energy spread throughout my entire body. It was such a vast amount of energy that if someone unable to withstand it consumed it, their meridians might burst instead.

But with meridians strengthened by the Heavenly Meridian Reinforcement Art, and with the pure internal energy accumulated through the elixirs I had already taken, I was able to dissolve the Ten-Thousand-Year Black Lingzhi without any side effects.

I circulated my true qi once, twice, and three times, focusing wholeheartedly on dissolving its energy. After storing that energy in my dantian, I opened my eyes.

I could not know how deep the internal energy of my father, the Martial Alliance Leader, or the Evil Dao Alliance Leader was, but if one considered internal energy alone, I could now confidently say I was not inferior to the three of them.

I left the letter and Patriarch Mansa’s teachings on the wall as they were. When the earthquake came and the wall collapsed, most of it would be damaged anyway, so there was no need for me to erase Patriarch Mansa’s teachings with my own hands.

I crawled out of that place and put the stone slab I had cut out back where it had been.

Since there was still time left before the promised two hours, I read the section about the final battle between the Heavenly Demon and Patriarch Mansa.

In the battle between Patriarch Mansa and the Heavenly Demon, the Heavenly Demon Soul had manifested. Since the painting had been drawn by people of the Evil Path, they had depicted the Heavenly Demon Soul like a demon.

I stared closely at the Heavenly Demon Soul.

If one achieved grand completion of the Nine Flames Demonic Art, one could manifest the Heavenly Demon Soul.

Would I, too, one day be able to create a Heavenly Demon Soul? What form would my Heavenly Demon Soul take when it manifested? Would it appear in a smaller form instead of such a gigantic one?

Just thinking about it made my heart pound.

It was then.

Swoosh. For a moment, I had the illusion that the Heavenly Demon Soul in the painting was looking at me.

Startled, I looked at the Heavenly Demon Soul again, but it remained exactly as it was within the painting.

In truth, this was the second time I had experienced something like this.

Long ago, when my father had pressured me with demonic energy, I had once felt as if something enormous in the abyss was looking at me.

This felt exactly like that.

Was I seeing illusions because of my desperate longing for the Nine Flames Demonic Art?

“Next time, let us meet not as a painting, but when I manifest you myself.”

After saying farewell to the Heavenly Demon Soul like that, I left Mansa Cave.

Bisain was waiting outside. He examined me with eyes full of curiosity. But with his level, he could not discern any change.

“I read it well. It truly is a place worthy of being called a holy site. Patriarch Mansa was an extraordinary man.”

“Do not think of coming here ever again.”

“It may be a holy site to you people, but to me it is only a suffocating underground cave. Seeing it once was enough. Now, let us leave.”

We went outside through the same pond by which we had entered.

Emerging beneath the waterfall, we soared up the cliff. Unlike me, who rose in a single bound, he kicked off the cliff once midway before reaching the top.

We began running toward the main headquarters of the Evil Dao Alliance.

After running without rest like that, we decided to stop for a short break. As we sat side by side on a hill and rested, Bisain spoke worriedly.

“By now, there must be quite an uproar. The successor of the Evil Dao Alliance has gone missing, after all. And together with the child of the Heavenly Demon, no less.”

“There may be less trouble than you think.”

At that, Bisain looked at me and asked,

“Why do you think so?”

“Soma will have hinted something to First Wolf. He will have told him that nothing dangerous would happen, so there is no need to make a fuss.”

“Did you instruct the Extreme Evil Soma to do that?”

I shook my head.

“Sometimes there are things one can understand without being told, are there not?”

“Since becoming successor, this is the first time I have been separated from the Evil Dao Thirteen Wolves. And yet I cannot predict at all how they will move.”

He could not hide his bitter expression. Since I had induced their division, I felt a little sorry toward him.

“How does it feel to be alone?”

“I do not know yet. I thought it would be very uncomfortable, but I do not feel particularly uncomfortable or anxious.”

“That is because you are with me. I have a charm that puts people at ease.”

At my shameless words, Bisain wore a look of disbelief. After spending time with him, I could read the subtle changes in his expression. At first, every expression had seemed like the same distorted face, but when one looked closely, there were differences.

“I can read your expressions now, Young Master Bi.”

When I brought up his expression, he flinched. Just as a mask was a reverse scale to the Extreme Evil Soma, Bisain’s facial scars were like a reverse scale to him. But I did not hesitate to touch on that point.

“Later, when you gain a subordinate you can trust, show that person your expressions in detail. Only then will he be able to read your emotions. In fact, the closer the relationship, the more that person respects you, the less likely they may be able to read your expression. Do not make that mistake.”

“Why are you giving me such advice?”

“Because I hope the Evil Dao Alliance runs well. Because I hope our cult, your alliance, and the Martial Alliance maintain balance. If that balance collapses… you know, do you not? What will happen.”

“Is that truly all?”

“Do you think I was ever taught to trust people? Even so, when I must trust, I do.”

“I simply cannot understand why you would take such risks.”

“The reason is simple. He tried to kill us. He tried to use Lord Soma. That is reason enough to kill him. But do not believe I will help you to the very end. We are not greenhorns fresh to the jianghu and mad with vengeance. If the situation looks wrong, we will immediately pull out. This matter is, in the end, your problem, Young Master Bi.”

Bisain seemed troubled.

“What I should do… To be honest, I am somewhat confused.”

The Bisain I had first met and the Bisain now were clearly different. Wounded pride tends to blind a person. But the current Bisain clearly had a stronger will to overcome reality than to cling to his pride.

“Is it not simple?”

“What do you mean?”

“You only need to repay them exactly as they did to you. He tried to kill you, so you kill him as well. He did not try to seize power by killing the Evil Dao Alliance Leader, but tried to kill you, the successor, so you likewise kill his grandson. Cast aside your fear and awe of Seok Gwanchu. He is nothing but an ugly old man coveting power. Evidence or whatever else—throw it all away and simply kill him.”

Bisain nodded firmly.

“You are right. This problem was truly simple.”

It seemed something that had been tightly blocked inside him had opened.

“I will kill them all. I will kill everyone who stands in my way as well.”

“Of course you should. Do not make a simple problem complicated.”

After looking at me for a moment, Bisain asked,

“Can you still read my expression?”

“I can.”

“How is it?”

“You are happy.”

“So I truly did have expressions.”

“You did not know?”

“I did not. Everyone was too busy avoiding my face.”

“Not everyone. You were. Stop breaking mirrors now.”

A deeper smile formed on the face that normally would have flared up in anger. I stared at that face for a while, then rose first.

“Now then, let us run!”

Chapter 114: I Tried to Lure in an Evildoer.
--------------------------------------------
I arrived with Bi Sa-in at one of their safe houses not far from the Sado Alliance’s main headquarters.

Geugak Soma and the Thirteen Sado Wolves were waiting there.

Ilrang rejoiced as if a dead man had returned.

“Young Master! You’re safe!”

“Ilrang, I’m sorry I left without telling you in advance.”

“I was terribly worried. You must never do this again.”

“I understand.”

The other Thirteen Sado Wolves were glad as well. Seeing them welcome him seemed to ease Bi Sa-in’s heart.

“Demon Venerable Geugak did guarantee the young master’s safety, but…”

The thought of Ilrang wondering whether a guarantee from Geugak Soma might not make things even more dangerous made me let out a small laugh.

While Bi Sa-in and the Thirteen Sado Wolves were reunited, Geugak Soma called me aside into a room.

The two eyes behind the white mask scrutinized me from head to toe.

“Bi Sa-in probably doesn’t even know what was taken from him. Just what did you gain?”

“I simply tried to win that man’s heart.”

“That can’t be it. That much could have been done even if I or the Thirteen Sado Wolves had been there.”

After traveling together all this time, Geugak Soma had come to understand me far too well.

“Now that I look at you, your gaze seems to have deepened too. Did you perhaps make progress in martial arts?”

“A very little.”

In truth, reaching great completion of the Twelfth Stage of the Flying Heaven Sword Art was an enormous achievement, but I spoke only half the truth. I didn’t think I could deceive Geugak Soma completely, and I didn’t want to deceive him completely either.

“If the Second Young Master’s skills have improved even further, then if we fought now, I would lose.”

“I might not know about the other Demon Venerables, but I don’t think I could defeat you, Soma-nim.”

“Why not?”

“Because you are Geugak Soma.”

Knowing that I was praising him, Geugak Soma laughed.

That afternoon, Bi Sa-in came to see me.

He had come up with a plan, and it was extremely dangerous.

“I intend to use myself as bait.”

He was staking his life on this matter.

“I will leak word that I possess evidence. Evidence proving that Seok Gwan-chu was behind all the deeds committed thus far. If I say I will soon report it to the Alliance Leader, Seok Gwan-chu will surely come to kill us.”

Heuksalju had died, we had gone to Baegya Valley, and even Yukrang had died. It was a situation in which it was plausible that we had evidence.

“He has to kill us as quickly as possible, but he won’t be able to procure an outside expert in a hurry. Either he will come himself, or an expert deeply connected to Seok Gwan-chu will.”

“Do you know how dangerous this plan is?”

“I know. He will certainly send someone capable of killing me. He may even come himself.”

“Then why devise such a dangerous plan?”

“Because there is one thing they are overlooking.”

“What is it?”

“You, Second Young Master. They will never imagine that you and Geugak Soma will help me.”

He asked me.

“Will you help me?”

“I will.”

Bi Sa-in was instead taken aback by my answer, given without the slightest hesitation.

“Why are you not demanding a price? This is precisely the sort of situation where I would have no choice but to accept whatever conditions you set. Even if you told me to give you everything I own, I would have no choice but to do it.”

“Because I want to properly shake your hand one day.”

Bi Sa-in’s eyes wavered. Help received in difficult times always left the deepest mark. If I won Bi Sa-in’s heart this time, it would become a power greater than any elixir or treasure.

After parting with him, I met Geugak Soma and told him about this plan.

“Second Young Master, stay out of it.”

“Are you serious?”

“Yes.”

“Because you’re worried about me?”

“No, because I’m worried about myself. I came out here with the Second Young Master, so if you die in the front yard of the Sado Alliance, what would that make me? I would have no face to show the Cult Leader.”

In truth, I had the exact same worry. Father had ordered me not to kill any more Demon Venerables, so if Geugak Soma were to die by some chance, what would that make me?

“Let’s decide after we see how things unfold.”

“Very well.”

“This is not our fight. There’s no need to throw ourselves into too much danger.”

At my words, Geugak Soma’s eyes instead began to shine. The stronger the opponent, the more fiercely he burned.

After returning to my room, I wound the supreme Heavenly Silkworm Silk that had been wrapped around my sword hilt around my heart, neck, abdomen, and the vital parts of my limbs. Over it, I put on the Ghost Tiger Garment that the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had given me.

Then I quietly circulated my energy and regulated my breathing, preparing for the battle to come.

Late that night, I opened my eyes.

The Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art had awakened me. This was the first time it had ever awakened me so violently and forcefully. I could tell.

‘A powerful enemy.’

I had expected a powerful enemy to come, but this was the first time an expert had made the Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art react so strongly.

Geugak Soma, who had been sleeping in the same room, snapped his eyes open as well.

“Did you feel it?”

“Yes.”

“Let’s go out.”

Like Geugak Soma, I put on a mask.

We headed toward the place from which that aura was flowing. It was the building where Bi Sa-in was staying.

Crack! Crash!

Warriors burst out, shattering the windows and walls. They were none other than Bi Sa-in and the Thirteen Sado Wolves. Their shock was unmistakable.

And soon after, an old man appeared, walking out at a leisurely pace.

Dressed in pure white, he had a white beard that flowed down to his chest like an immortal. His face and hands were lined with wrinkles whose age was impossible to fathom, yet his eyes alone were as terrifying as those of a wild beast.

The old man was dragging someone along. The man was already a corpse, and the place where he was dragged was smeared thickly with blood.

Whoosh.

When the old man threw the corpse, it flew into the middle of the courtyard and rolled. The dead man was Samrang.

At the death of their comrade, the expressions of the Thirteen Sado Wolves hardened. A murderous intent of vengeance filled their eyes, but none cursed or acted rashly.

They had clearly been suppressed by the swordsmanship the old man had shown when killing Samrang.

“Who are you?”

Ilrang suppressed his anger and asked the old man.

He was not Seok Gwan-chu. He was older than Seok Gwan-chu, who was an expert of the previous generation, and he exuded a far more intense presence.

“And who might you be?”

The old man’s voice split the night air. It was low and calm, yet one could feel profound inner power within it.

“I am Ilrang, leader of the Thirteen Sado Wolves.”

“Come closer and let me have a look.”

Of course, Ilrang did not approach.

“How does a coward lead the Thirteen Sado Wolves?”

Ilrang’s face stiffened, but he did not fall for the old man’s provocation.

Instead, he spoke quietly to Bi Sa-in.

“We will handle this place, so please escape.”

The plan to lure them in and capture them collapsed in an instant with the old man’s appearance.

Bi Sa-in had no intention of escaping, but even if he had tried, it would not have been easy. Another expert had appeared atop the wall on the opposite side.

It was Seok Gwan-chu, the mastermind behind this affair. He too displayed the bearing of an expert of the previous generation.

The fact that he had appeared in person meant he intended to kill everyone and silence them.

With his hands clasped behind his back, Seok Gwan-chu looked up at the night sky and said,

“The moonlight was lovely, so I brought my master out for a brief stroll.”

The instant everyone learned the old man’s identity, they were shocked.

Seok Gwan-chu’s master.

Sword Emperor Baek Mang-gi.

A master of swift swordsmanship, he was an expert of the generation before the previous one, already well over a hundred and thirty years old.

He, who had not shown himself in the murim for decades, had now appeared. We had tried to draw out a villain, but a monster had been dragged out instead.

“Listen, Young Master Bi.”

Seok Gwan-chu was calm and composed.

“There is only one way for you to live.”

“What is it?”

“You know what I want, do you not?”

“You mean the evidence I intend to present to the Alliance Leader? It seems even Elder Seok is afraid of the Alliance Leader.”

“Hand it over while I am asking nicely.”

“Do you think I am a fool? Why would I be carrying it on me? If you kill me, the evidence will go to the Alliance Leader that very instant. Then Elder Seok will have to bear the crime of killing me as well.”

At that moment, Seok Gwan-chu’s master, Baek Mang-gi, lightly launched himself forward. He slowly walked through the air and gently descended before Bi Sa-in. It was as if a divine general were alighting from the heavens.

Clang-clang-clang!

Every sword that tried to protect Bi Sa-in was deflected.

Baek Mang-gi’s sword was back in its sheath. In that brief instant, he had drawn his sword, knocked away the swords of the Thirteen Sado Wolves, and returned it.

The Thirteen Sado Wolves were overwhelmed by that swift swordsmanship.

Baek Mang-gi silently looked into Bi Sa-in’s eyes.

“Those are eyes that do not know how to lie.”

Baek Mang-gi looked at Seok Gwan-chu and said,

“There is no evidence.”

“I thought as much.”

As if he would believe anything his master said, Seok Gwan-chu rebuked Bi Sa-in with a composed face.

“Just what did you trust in to do such a thing? Did you think I looked like a fool who would walk into the trap you dug and offer up my neck?”

Bi Sa-in said nothing. The moment Baek Mang-gi came, every plan had been thrown into chaos.

Seok Gwan-chu could be faced if the Thirteen Sado Wolves joined forces, but Baek Mang-gi was an existence far beyond their abilities.

Then Baek Mang-gi turned his head and looked this way.

“It seems you trusted those fellows playing at being ghosts over there.”

Naturally, Baek Mang-gi and Seok Gwan-chu had been aware of our presence from the beginning. Seok Gwan-chu knew exactly who we were.

“He is Geugak Soma, one of the Eight Demon Venerables.”

“I have heard that the Demon Cult has underlings who wear mask shells and strut about pretending to be mysterious. But if they know who I am, why are they standing there like that? Do they think I will fear the Heavenly Demon and spare them?”

“It appears so.”

Then Geugak Soma said to me,

“It is not too late even now.”

“Should we run?”

“Do as you wish, Second Young Master.”

At that moment, my eyes met Bi Sa-in’s. Instead of fear, his expression held dignity. It said that it was all right for me to leave, that he would not resent me.

“I don’t want to remember that expression as the last thing I saw. Damn loyalty, pride, promises. Ah! This is why the average lifespan of murim people drops so sharply.”

“I knew you would say that.”

Geugak Soma laughed. Even if he were to die when the time came, dying while fighting like a madman was Geugak Soma’s way.

That was not why I decided to fight. It was because I knew they had no intention of simply letting us go. They would never allow such a great secret to leak outside.

“Hey, old man. Your opponent is over here.”

At Geugak Soma’s words, Baek Mang-gi laughed. Seok Gwan-chu laughed as well.

“Did you think I cared about the Heavenly Demon?”

The instant Baek Mang-gi finished speaking, he charged toward us. He was truly fast. The moment I perceived his attack as him coming at us, his sword was being drawn right before our noses.

Shiiik! Shiiik!

Piiit! Pat!

In an instant, it was over.

Baek Mang-gi had taken one step back from us and was staring at me with a startled face.

“You?”

His long beard had been cut and was fluttering between us.

Baek Mang-gi had tried to cut down Geugak Soma first, and I had cut at Baek Mang-gi. He was fast, but I was fast as well. Baek Mang-gi had nearly had his neck severed by my sword.

The ease vanished from Baek Mang-gi’s face. He must not have experienced such a dangerous moment in decades. It must have been the first time his heart had dropped like this.

Not only Baek Mang-gi, but Seok Gwan-chu, Bi Sa-in, and the Thirteen Sado Wolves all stared at me in astonishment.

It was right then.

Riiip.

The mask of Geugak Soma, struck by Baek Mang-gi’s earlier sword, split in half from forehead to chin and slid down.

Even Geugak Soma had failed to perfectly evade Baek Mang-gi’s attack.

As the mask slid away, Geugak Soma was flustered. If asked whether he would rather die or take off his mask, he was the kind of man who would choose death.

And just as his face was about to be revealed—

Clack.

A new mask covered his face.

I had taken off the mask I was wearing and blocked his face with it.

For an instant, our eyes met. Instead of the shame he would have hated more than death, his two eyes were filled with surging emotion toward me.

“Remove your mask only when you wish to, Soma-nim.”

Leaving behind Geugak Soma’s swelling emotions, I turned toward Baek Mang-gi.

I hadn’t known. I hadn’t known that the fact Geugak Soma had nearly died would make me this angry. Truly, I hadn’t known.

Chapter 115: A Lot of Blood
---------------------------
Baek Manggi’s face as he looked at me held many emotions.

Fear, surprise, excitement, rage.

Of them, the first to leap out was surprise.

“You’re this young?”

When he had nearly died to my sword at first, he must have thought I was the Geugak Soma. That he had been mistaken. If I wasn’t the Geugak Soma, then I wouldn’t have been able to swing a sword so quickly.

“Hey, old man.”

I dispensed with courtesy toward him. I did not know what kind of life he had lived. But the moment he cut down Samrang here today, any reason to respect him disappeared.

“Did you say we were playing ghost tricks? Aren’t you the one playing ghost right now?”

“What did you just say?”

“I’m asking why you crawled out of your grave to meddle in the affairs of later generations. You lived through your own era and even earned the name Sword Emperor. Was that not enough?”

When I struck the sore spot, the air around us turned cold.

But I was feeling a scorching heat. It was the heat of the Geugak Soma behind me, slowly putting his mask on properly. I did not need to look into his eyes to know what he felt.

Yes, there are things one can know without words, without even looking.

“Listen, young junior. What use is such foul speech toward your elders?”

“Old man, you’re going to kill me anyway, so isn’t it shameless to expect manners on top of that? Then again, if you had any shame, you wouldn’t have been cutting down those young juniors.”

As the surrounding air grew even colder, he stroked his severed beard. I fanned his killing intent even higher.

“If your disciple went senile and threw himself into a power struggle, you, his master, should have stopped him. What are you doing crawling out as if you’d been waiting for this? Have you both gone senile as a pair?”

Baek Manggi’s face flushed slightly. Yes, get worked up. Get even more worked up.

I shouted to Bisa-in and the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Path standing in the distance.

“Let’s send these old men back into history.”

It meant we would take care of this side, so they should handle Seok Gwanchu.

Hope bloomed in Bisa-in’s eyes, which had been filled with despair. If we took Baek Manggi, this fight was more than worth attempting.

On the other hand, Seok Gwanchu’s expression was stiff. He never could have imagined that a variable like this would appear in a matter for which he had even mobilized his master.

Baek Manggi asked coldly.

“Who are you, you bewitching wretch?”

“The one who will take an old man omitted from the register of the dead to the underworld.”

At that moment, Baek Manggi drew his sword with lightning speed.

Flash!

I barely dodged the sword that flew at me. Baek Manggi’s attack was fast and powerful.

At that instant!

Piiing!

The Geugak Soma’s Hyeorangji shot forth. Baek Manggi twisted his body to evade it, and my sword stabbed toward him.

As we clashed in an instant, the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Path and Bisa-in also rushed at Seok Gwanchu.

I resolved to fight without worrying about the Geugak Soma.

To begin with, I had never fought in coordination with him, and I judged that trying some clumsy combined assault against Baek Manggi would only put us at a disadvantage. I trusted the Geugak Soma.

He would take care of himself. He would intervene on his own, dodge on his own, help on his own. He would fight well on his own.

That was why I fought with the mindset that I was fighting Baek Manggi one-on-one.

Clang!

There was another clash. His swift sword was too fast for the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Path to receive. Even for the Geugak Soma, it would not be easy to dodge such attacks in succession.

If I saw it with my eyes and then blocked, it was already too late. I had to block with experience and instinct.

He’s attacking the left, so I should attack the right. The moment I thought something like that, my neck would be severed.

Baek Manggi’s techniques, which had insisted solely on speed, became dazzling. This sudden change was terribly threatening.

But I barely managed to block his attacks. I strove not to be suppressed by his aura.

As we exchanged techniques, the Geugak Soma’s Hyeorangji was released. Because our movements were so fast, the Hyeorangji had to be fired with extreme caution. One slight mistake and it might hit me.

Even though it was a two-on-one fight, Baek Manggi was not being pushed back at all. He was showing with his skill what it meant to have borne the name Sword Emperor eighty years ago.

His swordsmanship was one thing, but Baek Manggi’s footwork was also splendid. Had I not learned the Pungsin Four Steps, I would have been completely trampled by that footwork. To avoid being struck by the Hyeorangji, he fought as though tangled up with me, and every single step was astonishingly fluid.

After more than a hundred exchanges, Baek Manggi, who had been trading sword forms with me, suddenly unleashed palm force. It was truly an ever-changing attack, but fortunately, I was not late in releasing my own palm force to block it.

Boom!

As palm force met palm force, a thunderous roar exploded.

We both slid backward. Our inner power was almost equal. If not for the Ten-Thousand-Year Black Spirit Mushroom I had recently consumed, I would have been pushed back in internal energy.

When we had thus separated from each other, we had the leisure to speak.

“I do not wish to be surprised, yet I cannot help it. How in the world can you possess such profound inner power at that age?”

I spoke to him cheerfully.

“My friend, don’t you recognize me?”

“What?”

“I underwent Banrohwandong.”

For a moment, he thought I was someone he knew.

“Who?”

“Who else? Your foul-mouthed young junior. You don’t even have any friends, so why ask who I am?”

Only then did Baek Manggi realize I had mocked him. His expression twisted. Whenever I had the chance, I had to provoke him.

As always, in a fight, the side that gets agitated loses.

For the first time, sword qi burst from his agitated blade.

Swaaaaaak!

It was the moment I had been waiting for.

Boom!

The sword qi I released collided with the incoming sword qi, and an explosion rang out.

Not missing this opportunity, the Geugak Soma unleashed Hyeorangji and Mageuk Gwangpokjang in succession. Since it was a two-on-one fight, this battle was advantageous to us.

I poured out Yeomcheon, the sword qi release form, one after another.

The Geugak Soma likewise ceaselessly unleashed Hyeorangji and Mageuk Gwangpokjang.

Baek Manggi frantically swung his sword and released palm force to disperse them. Explosions rang out one after another.

Boom! Boom! Kwaaaang! Boom!

The ground shook, and dust rose into the air.

At this tremendous exchange, even Seok Gwanchu, Bisa-in, and the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Path stopped fighting and watched for the result.

When the attacks ceased and the dust settled, we were astonished.

He was standing unharmed.

And then came the Sword Emperor’s ultimate technique, created by rage.

Sssssssss!

Before him, his sword was splitting into sword-shaped sword qi.

I could tell. He intended to kill everyone here with a single move. He meant to kill the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Path and Bisa-in, then face us together with Seok Gwanchu.

The Black Demon Sword rose before my chest and began to split.

Sssssssssss!

The Seventh Form of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, Yucheon.

Was it because I had achieved great completion of the twelve stars? It split far faster than before, and into a far greater number.

Baek Manggi’s sword qi flew toward everyone here.

Shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik!

Swaaaaaaaaa!

At the same time, my sword qi was also released.

In the next instant, a magnificent sight unfolded.

Two kinds of lines embroidered the space. Lines meant to kill, and lines meant to protect.

The moment those two lines intersected, explosions from sword qi striking sword qi burst out in succession.

Bisa-in saw it. Baek Manggi’s sword qi flying toward his face. Its speed was so terrifying that even if he tried to dodge, he would not be able to.

But my sword qi flew in faster, tore that sword qi apart, and destroyed it right before his eyes.

Boom!

It was the same for the other Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Path. The sight of sword qi flying toward them being ripped apart by other sword qi was something they would hardly ever see again in their lives.

Boom! Boom! Boom! Kwaang! Boom!

The Geugak Soma destroyed the sword qi on his own. I trusted that he would, so I did not block the sword qi aimed at him.

When all the sword qi that had flown out disappeared, the dust had also completely settled.

Baek Manggi came into view. His eyes, mixed with disbelief, surprise, and rage, were filled only with killing intent. Seok Gwanchu was shocked and flustered. He had never imagined that his master’s secret ultimate technique would be blocked.

Conversely, the eyes of Bisa-in and the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Path as they looked at us were filled with astonishment. Beyond surprise, it was close to awe.

This time, I was the first to charge at Baek Manggi.

If you do your utmost, then I will give my all. If you die, only you die, but if I die, far too many people will die.

I poured everything into my sword.

In an instant, dozens of moves were exchanged.

Blood sprayed from both our bodies. There was no leisure to check where and how we had been cut. The fact that blood was flying even though I wore the finest Heavenly Silkworm Thread and the Ghost Tiger Robe meant that, had I not worn them, several parts of me might already have been cut off.

As I endured, and endured again, I sensed impatience from Baek Manggi.

When was the last time he had fought such a long bloody battle?

Fifty years ago? Seventy years ago? Perhaps he had never experienced such a difficult fight at all. After becoming the Sword Emperor, no one would have dared challenge him lightly.

In this fight, the Geugak Soma’s help was absolute. Because our battle was too fast, he did not intervene recklessly, but whenever he was certain, he fired Hyeorangji.

When I was in danger, Hyeorangji saved me. A single Hyeorangji arriving at the right moment changed the flow of the battle and gave me an opportunity.

In the end, Baek Manggi changed his plan and tried to kill the Geugak Soma first.

The instant I sensed his killing intent toward the Geugak Soma, I drove Baek Manggi back like a madman.

If I gave him an opening, the Geugak Soma would die.

That was my only thought.

I did not want to lose the Geugak Soma in this fight. I did not want him to die to this old man.

If anyone kills him, it’ll be me! You damned old man!

That desperate desire created one astonishing phenomenon.

Everything vanished from my field of vision, and only Baek Manggi remained.

It was as if, against a black background, only Baek Manggi stood before me in white robes, moving alone. I could see nothing else.

All sound was cut off as well. I could hear only my own sounds and the sounds Baek Manggi made. It was an astonishing experience I had never undergone since learning martial arts.

At the moment Baek Manggi’s sword and mine collided, scattering sparks.

There was another change.

Now even Baek Manggi disappeared, and in my eyes, only his sword moved. Only my sword was visible. Perhaps, if more time passed, even these swords would cease to be seen.

Baek Manggi’s sword began to appear more and more clearly.

My vision, brightened by the Divine Eye Art, had finally begun to take effect. It was not that he had become slower, nor that I had become faster. I was the one who had adapted to this speed first. My eyes had adapted.

Only the swords had been visible, but now Baek Manggi’s figure appeared again.

I could see his face.

Now, at last, I could see.

That he was struggling.

That he regretted.

That he feared.

And I saw it.

The single opening shown by the man who had been perfect.

The moment I recognized it, my sword had already put my will into action. My sword and body were one.

Thud!

The moment my sword pierced Baek Manggi’s heart.

My vision returned to normal. As the surrounding darkness vanished, I began to see my surroundings.

There were already several holes in Baek Manggi’s body, and blood was flowing from them. They were the places Hyeorangji had struck.

I did not know when Baek Manggi had been hit by them. Even while facing the two of us, he had fought this well.

Looking down at his final gaze, which held surprise and shock, emptiness and fear, and every other emotion, I slowly pulled out my sword. I was so exhausted and drained that I had nothing to say to him.

Blood gushed from his heart, and Baek Manggi collapsed, dead.

Had I not achieved great completion of the twelve stars, I would have lost this fight. Had I not fought together with the Geugak Soma, we would have died.

In murim, there exist monstrous masters of the previous generation like this. They are in the Murim Alliance, they are in our cult, and they are also in the Evil Path Alliance like this.

Old masters who lie hardened like fossils, then abruptly spring out and try to flare with their final flame. That is why murim is frightening.

“Ah, I’m dying.”

I flopped down where I stood. The drain on my mind had been so great that I did not even have the strength to move a finger. Unless someone actually thrust a sword at me, I felt I could not move. The groan truly escaped me on its own.

The Geugak Soma also plopped down beside me. Beneath my mask, I could see his chin. I saw blood dried beneath it.

“Are you hurt?”

“It’s just a light scratch.”

“You’re probably ugly already, so you can’t afford to injure your face!”

As if he had never expected me to say such a thing in this situation, he gave a faint laugh.

“After fighting that hard, you still want to make jokes?”

“When an opportunity comes, you shouldn’t let it pass.”

“Second Young Master, you’re bleeding a lot.”

“I’m in so much pain I could die.”

Geugak Soma pressed my blood points, staunching the bleeding in my arms, legs, abdomen, and shoulder.

When we first set out on this journey, could I ever have imagined a moment would come when he would be stopping my wounds from bleeding?

“What happened to the fight over there?”

At my question, Geugak Soma glanced over toward the place where the battle was still raging, then said to me,

“It’s more or less ending.”

Seeing as he wasn’t saying we needed to help, it seemed Bi Sa-in’s side was winning.

For a while, we looked up at the sky in silence.

Then I spoke first.

“Thank you.”

After a brief pause, Geugak Soma said,

“Thank you.”

We only thanked each other. We added nothing more. There was no need to.

“I’ll buy you a new mask.”

“Please buy me a better one. That mask made me sweat if I wore it too long.”

“I will.”

It was right then.

“Kraaaagh!”

A scream rang out—the kind that comes when someone’s life is cut short.

When I lifted my head, I saw Bi Sa-in’s sword and the swords of the three Sado Thirteen Wolves buried in Seok Gwan-chu’s body.

During the fight, it seemed two more of the Sado Thirteen Wolves had been sacrificed; they lay collapsed on the ground. The wicked never go to the underworld alone.

I pushed myself up and sat.

Bi Sa-in walked toward me. He stopped a short distance away and stared at Baek Mang-gi’s corpse. It seemed he could hardly believe we had killed him.

He bowed his head to me. For the first time, he bowed to me.

“Thanks to you, I survived.”

I smiled and said to him,

“Thanks to you, I nearly died.”

He did not smile. Still holding his sword, blood dripping steadily from it, he spoke with eyes full of resolve.

“I intend to go straight from here to kill Seok Gwan-chu’s grandson.”

He was more courteous than when he had dealt with me before. And he was taking my advice well.

“The testimony of our Sado Thirteen Wolves should be enough evidence.”

“Be sure to make it a Sado Alliance where that will hold true.”

“Then I will see you next time.”

Bi Sa-in bowed his head. The Bi Sa-in I had first met and the Bi Sa-in now were completely different people.

After they collected their comrades’ bodies and left, Geugak Soma asked,

“For the sake of our cult, shouldn’t we be placing an even bigger fool than Bi Sa-in as the successor?”

“I suppose so.”

“Then why did you help that man?”

It was something that had begun because Seok Gwan-chu’s grandson was a piece of trash, but to Geugak Soma I answered like this.

“Still, if the opponent is too much of a fool, it isn’t any fun, is it?”

Geugak Soma gave a faint snort of laughter. He must have thought the same. He was the kind of person who burned hotter the stronger his opponent was.

“Shall we go as well?”

“Ahhh, my leg hurts too much to walk. Please carry me.”

“Stop exaggerating. You’re limping on the other leg now.”

Geugak Soma and I walked side by side and left that place.

In the place we left behind, only the faint moonlight shone upon the sight of an old, and even older, desire cooling coldly away.

Chapter 116: Why Are You Whispering?
------------------------------------
The woman diligently hammering away with a sledgehammer was none other than Ian. She was working in a small smithy that made farming tools like hoes and sickles.

Gifted with her hands, she had quickly learned the work and was now turning out hoes of quite decent quality with practiced ease.

This was one of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s safe houses. There were various kinds of safe houses: some hidden away where no human foot ever reached, and others like this one, disguised as ordinary places in areas where many people came and went.

Just then, someone entered.

“Eat before you keep working.”

When Ian turned, she saw that the Mistress of Cheonhwaru had brought her a meal.

“Thank you.”

The smithy work was mainly handled by Ian, while meals were the Mistress of Cheonhwaru’s responsibility. Cheongmyeon wore a mask, so he did not show himself and moved about in secret.

Looking at the Mistress of Cheonhwaru’s attire, Ian asked,

“Are you going somewhere?”

Normally, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru dressed like an ordinary married woman and even wore a headscarf. But today she had put on makeup and was dressed in beautiful clothes.

“No, I just wore them for a change of mood.”

As long as they were in a safe house, the rule was that going out was absolutely forbidden.

Ian could tell. Today was some special day for her.

“Is today your birthday, by any chance?”

“No. I just had a good dream. Now, eat before it gets cold.”

“Yes, thank you for the meal.”

Ian began using her chopsticks.

“Please eat with me.”

“I picked at a few bites while cooking, so I’m full. Eat plenty.”

“I really think you were born with a talent for cooking. It tastes much better than anything bought outside.”

“Actually, when I first began this work, I worked in the kitchen.”

“Ah, I see.”

“I suffered quite a lot back then.”

Ian could sense it. The experience of struggling from the very bottom was what had made this woman successful.

The food was delicious, but Ian could not eat much. Ever since coming here, she had had no appetite at all because she was worried about Geom Mugeuk.

“Did you hear what I said to the Second Young Master?”

“Yes. I heard you told him I was someone destined to do great things. Why did you say that?”

“I have a good eye for people.”

“Thank you for thinking so highly of me. But that praise is far more than I deserve.”

Gazing at Ian with calm eyes, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru said,

“You are a precious person, Warrior Ian. So always hold yourself precious.”

Ian was grateful to her for saying that.

While hiding in the safe house, Ian had not shown it outwardly, but she had been very worried about Geom Mugeuk. There were nights when she worried so much she could not sleep. Each time, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru comforted her, telling her nothing would happen.

Her comfort strangely gave strength. Perhaps it was because of the mysterious aura around the woman called the Mistress of Cheonhwaru, something difficult to explain in words.

Just then, Cheongmyeon, who rarely showed himself, entered.

Ian asked in surprise,

“What happened?”

“They say an emergency has been declared at the Evil Dao Alliance headquarters.”

“You don’t know the details?”

“No. Only the news that an emergency was declared has come in.”

Struggling to calm her anxious heart, Ian said,

“The Young Master will be all right. Lord Soma is protecting him.”

It was then. A welcome voice came from behind her.

“Why don’t you ever think of the opposite? I might be protecting Lord Soma.”

When she turned, Geom Mugeuk and Geugak Soma were walking in side by side. Blood was spattered across both their clothes, and Geom Mugeuk’s clothes in particular were soaked with it. Startled, Ian ran over and examined Geom Mugeuk’s body first.

“Are you all right? Are you hurt?”

Geom Mugeuk sank down onto the floor, and Ian checked his body. He had sword wounds here and there, but he had avoided any fatal injury.

“I’m badly hurt. My arms and legs were almost cut off!”

“…It doesn’t seem that bad.”

“No, look carefully. Check whether all my fingers and toes are still attached.”

Ian turned her head and bowed respectfully to Geugak Soma.

“Thank you for saving our Young Master.”

“I told you, that’s not it! It was the opposite!”

Geom Mugeuk joked, while Geugak Soma remained composed. Naturally, it looked as though Geugak Soma had saved Geom Mugeuk.

Geugak Soma was silently gazing at Geom Mugeuk. The Mistress of Cheonhwaru could feel the change in him. Just as the mask he wore had changed, the look in his eyes when he looked at Geom Mugeuk had changed as well.

She linked arms with Geugak Soma and asked,

“Brother, you aren’t hurt anywhere, are you?”

“No.”

“Then may we return to Cheonhwaru now?”

Geugak Soma nodded, and the Mistress of Cheonhwaru rejoiced.

Just as she had sensed the change in Geugak Soma, Ian was sensing one thing as well. Perhaps the Mistress of Cheonhwaru had known that the two of them would return today.

“Will you all be leaving right away?”

“We’ll go first.”

At Geugak Soma’s gesture with his chin, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru looked back and saw that Geom Mugeuk had fallen asleep while leaning against Ian’s back.

Ian gestured for them to go ahead, saying she would stay like this for a while.

The Mistress of Cheonhwaru whispered to Geugak Soma,

“Have a drink with food I made, for the first time in a while.”

“Sounds good.”

Geugak Soma, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru, and Cheongmyeon left first.

Ian sat still so that Geom Mugeuk would not wake. Only then did the expression on Ian’s face, which had been tense for so long, finally relax.

‘You came back safely, Young Master.’

* * *

I had a dream.

In the dream, I was fighting Baek Manggi. It was not a new fight, but a reenactment of the fight from before.

Baek Manggi’s sword techniques, which had been too fast to see while fighting, unfolded like a slow painting. My own techniques were moving slowly as well.

I felt as though I had become a third party, objectively watching that fight. As if I were a spectator looking on from the side.

The techniques I had thought I had forgotten because they had flashed past in an instant came back to me with precision. Like go players replaying a match, I replayed the fight. My subconscious had remembered the battle with Baek Manggi exactly.

I could even control the speed of the fight. The moment I thought I wanted it to stop here for a moment, Baek Manggi and I in the dream stopped moving.

Thanks to that, I was able to examine the position and angle of the swords in detail. The fight moved according to my will. I could watch it move slowly, or I could stop it.

In that way, I was able to compare how the Sword Emperor’s martial arts differed from mine. This process gave me an enlightenment as great as the experience of fighting him directly.

In truth, there had been a new experience even during the fight itself. That final moment when I fought Baek Manggi, when only he was visible and the world went dark.

I could tell. These mysterious occurrences were changes brought about because the Flying Heaven Sword Art had reached great completion of the twelfth stage, my internal energy had surpassed a certain limit, and on top of that, the teachings of Mansa Jongju had been added.

The moment my sword pierced his heart for the last time, I woke from sleep.

My body and mind felt refreshed.

One might think fighting even in a dream would make me more tired, but that was not the case. It had been a dream just before waking, and it seemed I had slept to my heart’s content.

“Young Master, you’re awake? You must be hungry.”

“How long did I sleep?”

“Almost ten shichen.”

That meant I had slept nearly a full day. It had been a truly long time since I had slept so deeply. Come to think of it, this was the first time since my return that I had slept this well.

My physical condition was good too. Thanks to the Ghost Tiger Garment and the highest-grade Heavenly Silkworm Thread, the wounds I had suffered were not very deep.

‘Thank you, Elder.’

If the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had not given me the Ghost Tiger Garment, I would certainly have been hurt far worse. I unwound the highest-grade Heavenly Silkworm Thread and wrapped it back around the hilt of the Black Demon Sword.

“It doesn’t feel like I slept that long.”

“You must have been very tired.”

“What about Geugak Soma? Did he go to Cheonhwaru?”

“Yes, the two of them left first.”

Seeing how the thought suddenly occurred to me that I wanted to see Geugak Soma, it seemed true after all that people grew close by fighting together.

“Oh, right. New news came in while you were asleep. Bi Sain killed Seok Gwanchu’s grandson.”

I nodded silently. It was the moment Bi Sain’s fate had changed, along with the fate of the Evil Dao Alliance. Originally, the position of heir to the Evil Dao Alliance had been taken by Seok Gwanchu’s grandson. How this change would affect our future, I could not know.

“This is a good thing, right?”

“Probably?”

I flopped back down.

Ian pulled at the blanket.

“No. Eat first, then sleep.”

“Just a little longer.”

“I’ll prepare the meal, so come out as soon as I call you.”

I called Ian as she was leaving the room.

“Ian.”

“Yes?”

“It’s good to see you again.”

Ian smiled brightly.

“Me too.”

* * *

Chief Strategist Sama Myeong had come down to the operations command room.

The place known to be even safer than the Heavenly Demon Hall was this operations command room of Tongcheongak.

Hundreds of dispatches flew in each day and were analyzed here.

“Still nothing?”

“No.”

Because of the last dispatch that had flown in, Sama Myeong was tense.

Even when the news had arrived that Seok Gwanchu was behind it, he had not been this tense.

That was because he believed that Geom Mugeuk and Geugak Soma would manage on their own.

But the moment Baek Manggi’s name came in, an emergency was declared in Tongcheongak. If Baek Manggi had truly moved, then even the lives of Geom Mugeuk and Geugak Soma could not be guaranteed.

And yet the Cult Leader had given no order. Considering who the opponent was, it would be unreasonable to immediately dispatch experts nearby to support them, but he could have sent an urgent message telling Geom Mugeuk and Geugak Soma to stop everything and return. But Geom Ujin had not done so.

Sama Myeong thought it was indeed like the Cult Leader, yet at the same time thought that if it had been him, he could never have made such a decision.

‘Does he trust them that much?’

Since it was rare for the Chief Strategist to come down here and personally wait for a dispatch, the strategists working here were also extremely tense.

The strategists who managed this place were people with authority to handle Tongcheongak’s highest secrets. They were also the people Sama Myeong trusted most.

Just then, the dispatch they had been waiting for arrived. The strategist who checked its contents reported in a pleased tone.

“Both Seok Gwanchu and Baek Manggi are dead.”

At his subordinate’s report, Sama Myeong felt relieved, but inwardly he was greatly shocked.

“And our two?”

“They say the Second Young Master and Geugak Soma are both safe.”

Sama Myeong let out a sigh of relief and collapsed into his seat. With the tension leaving him, he leaned back in his chair and closed his eyes for a moment.

‘The two of them killed Baek Manggi and Seok Gwanchu? That can’t be.’

Since the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao and Bi Sain had moved together, there must have been other variables. A new expert from the Evil Dao Alliance’s side might have stepped forward as well. Once time passed, he would learn the details of what had happened.

In the meantime, the strategist who had made the report organized the dispatch and brought it as a written report. It was the report to be sent to the Heavenly Demon.

Receiving the report, Sama Myeong asked him,

“What do you think of this matter?”

“I don’t know how Baek Manggi died, but one thing is certain. Recently, news related to the Second Young Master always startles me.”

Sama Myeong nodded as if in agreement and rose from his seat.

But thinking that, at least for today, the Cult Leader—who seemed almost to have predicted this outcome—was even more astonishing, Sama Myeong left the operations room.

* * *

A few days after arriving at the safe house, I went around the marketplace and bought various items. There were many things to buy, so I bought a cart and loaded them onto it.

First, I stopped by a physician’s office and bought several new needle cases. Then I went to an apothecary and purchased a large quantity of various medicinal herbs. Some were precious and expensive, while others were commonly used.

After gathering the medicinal herbs, I bought a tub. It was a large wooden tub big enough for three or four people to stand inside.

I bought new blankets and clothes, as well as enough food and alcohol to last a while. After that, I even bought a large bronze mirror that could reflect the whole body.

When Ian saw the things I had brought back from the market, she was startled.

“What is all this?”

“Try guessing.”

“They have no connection to each other at all.”

Looking at the herbs piled high, she asked me with a worried expression,

“You didn’t suffer internal injuries, did you?”

“I didn’t tell you because I thought you’d worry. I think I need to hurry and see the Divine Physician.”

Ian examined the cart again.

“You’re not hurt. But why did you buy so many medicinal herbs?”

Then she picked something up from the cart.

“What is this? Gasp!”

She put what she had picked up back where it belonged, crouched beside the cart, and whispered,

“These are women’s undergarments!”

“That’s right.”

I crouched down just like her.

“Why did you buy undergarments?”

“Because I need them. But why are you suddenly whispering? Did an enemy appear?”

“Well… because something even more shocking than a treasure map came out of the cart.”

“My back hurts. Can’t we stand up now?”

“There are other clothes too! They’re so pretty!”

There was also a beautiful palace dress made of high-quality silk.

“Who are they for? They’re for a very slender woman.”

“There’s someone I’m going to give them to.”

“If you’re giving undergarments as a gift, she must be someone extremely close to you.”

“She is close.”

“There’s no way I wouldn’t know someone that close to you. But it can’t be the Mistress of Cheonhwaru. Ah! Did you perhaps find a courtesan you liked among the women of Cheonhwaru?”

“Want me to show you who it is?”

“Really?”

“Of course. Let’s go.”

Together with Ian, I pulled the cart to a modest manor located far from the village.

“Is the person living here?”

“You could say that.”

But when we entered, the manor was empty.

“There’s no one here. It doesn’t seem like anyone lives here.”

“There will be now. I rented it for a few days.”

“Why?”

To her, who looked puzzled, I spoke. The words that would change her life, the words she most wanted to hear, flowed from my mouth.

“Because I intend to perform the Divine Poison Purification Art on you here.”

Chapter 117: Your Eyes Will Swell, Don't Cry.
---------------------------------------------
Ian was startled.

She had been surprised many times after meeting me, but this was the first time I had ever seen her so shocked.

Her two eyes, which had been hidden beneath layers of flesh, began to tremble.

“Ian, the day has finally come.”

She could not say a word. She had never shown it outwardly, but I did not need to ask how long she had waited for this moment.

“When will the procedure be performed?”

“Now.”
