“Really? Now? The moment I’ve been waiting for all this time just came so suddenly?”

“Things that change one’s fate always arrive unexpectedly. That’s why, when you look back, everything seems like luck or coincidence.”

“I’m trembling.”

“That’s all right. You’re allowed to tremble. I just can’t.”

I deliberately made a show of trembling as I pretended to misplace the needle. I was only joking to ease her tension, but Ian was so nervous and excited that she stared blankly, unable to tell whether I was joking or not.

I would have liked to open a splendid space with the Time-Space Transposition Art and perform the procedure there, but I could not, because maintaining the space would consume too much internal energy.

“Lie down on that bed.”

Ian lay down on the bed as instructed. She took several deep breaths. Until she had woken up that morning, she truly could never have dreamed that she would be lying there for the Divine Poison Purification Technique today.

I began inserting needles into the designated acupoints. With every needle, I performed the acupuncture with all my might. From the side, it might have looked like something any neighborhood physician could do, but despite appearances, the technique involved was of the highest order.

I had to inject the prescribed internal energy into each needle, and at times I had to recite incantations as I inserted them. A single mistake would cause the procedure to fail and inflict severe internal injuries upon her.

While inserting the needles, I periodically infused her body with internal energy to calm her rampaging qi and blood.

Heat poured from her body, and sweat streamed down her like rain. She groaned and whimpered beneath the onrushing pain.

“It hurts a lot, doesn’t it? That’s normal.”

She said something. She seemed to be saying that she could endure it, but I could not hear her clearly. The procedure was that difficult and painful.

I performed the procedure with care until the very last moment.

After inserting all the prescribed needles, I stepped outside for a while.

I quietly closed my eyes, sat cross-legged, and regulated my internal energy. It had truly consumed an enormous amount of mental strength.

Inserting the needles would not end after a single round.

After withdrawing every needle, I inserted them all again from the beginning, this time in a different order. As the procedure progressed, the strain on my mind grew greater, and so did the pain Ian endured.

“Uuuugh!”

A scream escaped her lips.

She was normally quite stoic and had an exceptional tolerance for pain, but this seemed to be more than even she could endure.

‘Hang in there, Ian.’

It pained me to watch, but she had no choice except to overcome it herself.

After the second round of acupuncture, I filled the tub I had bought with water, pounded the medicinal herbs, and mixed them into the water. The amount and the combination of herbs had to be exact.

Then I performed the acupuncture again.

This was the final ordeal for her as well. I did not know whether she could hear me, but I spoke softly to Ian.

“This is the last one. Endure it well.”

She gritted her teeth and bore the pain. I infused her with internal energy at intervals, gently soothing her qi and blood as I did everything I could to lessen her suffering.

‘Endure it, Ian.’

Only after the three grueling rounds of acupuncture were complete did I immerse her in the medicinal water. I connected a long tube to her throat and secured it so she could breathe.

Everything I could do was finished. All I could do now was pray that the procedure ended successfully.

* * *

Ian dreamed.

It was a dream of her earliest childhood.

“Can you learn the Whole Body Stone Transformation Art for the young master’s sake?”

She wanted to ask what the Whole Body Stone Transformation Art was, but it was not the kind of atmosphere in which she could ask. Her master had always been frightening.

“Yes.”

“There are side effects to learning the Whole Body Stone Transformation Art. Your body will swell.”

She did not fully understand what it meant to swell.

“The swelling will never go down. Ever.”

She did not understand what it meant for the swelling never to go down, either. She did not know how heavily that single word—ever—would weigh upon her life and make it so difficult. She was only eight years old at the time.

“Will you still do it?”

“Yes.”

The atmosphere made her feel that she could not say no.

But fear was not the only reason she had made that decision.

She wanted to remain by Geom Mugeuk’s side. She had liked him from the moment she first saw him.

Did she truly have no regrets?

Time passed within the dream.

She stood before a shattered bronze mirror. A strange girl in the broken mirror was glaring at her. The girl had grown so fat that Ian could no longer tell whether she was looking at herself or someone else, and she was angrily shouting.

‘Why are you angry? I’m the one who’s sad because of you.’

Time passed once more.

She watched Geom Mugeuk, drunk, from behind.

“Damn it!”

She knew what he wanted to say.

What do I lack compared to my brother?

That was what he could not bring himself to say aloud. The Cult Leader placed greater trust in the Eldest Young Master, and rumors openly circulated that the successor would be the Eldest Young Master.

After the Heavenly Demon Palace announced that anyone with the ability could become the successor, people began openly ignoring Geom Mugeuk. Some even thought that disregarding him would help them curry favor with the Eldest Young Master. Everyone believed that Geom Mugeuk would lose the succession struggle and die.

Only she knew how hard Geom Mugeuk tried to win his father’s approval.

Bang!

Geom Mugeuk struck the table. A wine bottle fell from the shattered table and broke.

She went over and cleared away the bottle.

Please stay strong, Young Master.

At some point, Geom Mugeuk had stopped giving her even a single warm look. His entire mind was consumed by the question of whether he would become the successor. Even so, she still liked him.

The dream carried her to another day.

The day when the fate that would change everything began.

Geom Mugeuk had returned after completing the Divine-Demon Tournament and was standing before a bronze mirror.

The look in his eyes as he gazed at her had changed.

Geom Mugeuk asked,

—How do I look?

It had been far too long since she had seen such a warm gaze. He must be in a good mood because he had won his match.

—You are the most handsome man in the martial world.

—How boring.

—The answer to a question from the person who gives me my monthly stipend is always predetermined.

When had they last exchanged jokes like that?

The Geom Mugeuk in the bronze mirror was staring intently at her.

—Why are you looking at me like that?

At that moment, she awoke from her dream and opened her eyes.

An unfamiliar ceiling came into view.

Only after some time had passed did she realize that she was in the place where the Divine Poison Purification Technique had been performed.

‘Is the Divine Poison Purification Technique over?’

She slowly raised herself. Her body felt different.

Then she looked at her hands.

They were slender, white, and long.

‘!’

Her heart dropped, then began pounding like a drum.

She squeezed her eyes shut.

‘Perhaps I’m still dreaming.’

Please, let this not be a dream!

With an anxious heart, she opened her eyes again. Her hands were no longer the ones that had been as large as a bear’s. Her dream had once been simply to become a little smaller, but now she possessed hands so beautiful and slender that they seemed carved from jade.

Suppressing the urge to scream, she slowly pulled back the blanket. The legs beneath it were unfamiliar. Her two legs, white and straight, were breathtakingly beautiful.

In the end, she screamed. It was the first time she had experienced how a person could scream involuntarily when overcome by joy.

She climbed down from the bed. The moment her feet touched the floor, fear washed over her that her legs might swell to their enormous former size and she might awaken from the dream.

But that did not happen. It was the moment she took the first step into her new life.

After climbing down from the bed, she slowly walked toward the bronze mirror.

“Ah!”

A strange woman stood within the mirror. She was unfamiliar, yet familiar. The features of her younger self were still there. How long had it been since she had seen her true appearance?

Her face was beautiful, and her figure was perfect.

How could such a single face possess both dazzling splendor and innocence? Her gentle eyes gave her an air of intelligence, while an aloofness that discouraged any careless approach could be felt in them.

No other description was necessary. In a single word, she was overwhelmingly beautiful.

She felt as though tears should pour from her eyes, but unexpectedly, they did not.

She was still dazed, unable to tell whether she was dreaming or awake.

Then she saw underwear and a white court robe laid beside the bed for her. The moment she saw them, her chest swelled with emotion, and she realized that none of this was a dream.

—Whose are these? They’re for an incredibly slender woman.

—There is someone I intend to give them to.

—If you’re giving her underwear as a gift, she must be someone very close to you.

—She is close.

The clothes Geom Mugeuk had prepared for someone close to him had been for her.

She put on the underwear and court robe, then stood before the bronze mirror once more. She was even more beautiful than before.

She was overjoyed that Geom Mugeuk had bought the clothes for this moment. His thoughtfulness toward her made her happier than she could express. At least for this moment, knowing that Geom Mugeuk had cared for her felt even better than having the side effects cured.

She slowly walked outside.

The person she had wanted so desperately to see stood in the courtyard with his back turned to her.

Ian slowly called out to him.

“Young Master.”

Geom Mugeuk slowly turned toward her.

He saw her standing there in her court robe. His eyes widened in an instant.

He had already seen her beautiful transformation when he finished the Divine Poison Purification Technique and laid her down on the bed. But the woman now standing before him with her eyes open was different from the one he had seen then. She was so beautiful that he was rendered speechless.

“The Divine Poison Purification Technique succeeded. Congratulations, Ian.”

Only then did tears begin to flow from her eyes.

“Your eyes will swell. Don’t cry on a day like this.”

“It’s all right if they swell. I lived with swelling for my whole life.”

“From now on, you must live differently.”

“No. My life hasn’t changed. Before you performed the Divine Poison Purification Technique and now, I’m still the same Ian.”

“Yes. To me, you are still the same Ian.”

Ian ran to Geom Mugeuk and threw her arms around him. She wept in his embrace for a long time. She did not say thank you. She felt that speaking the words aloud would diminish the gratitude in her heart.

“Can I ask you again?”

“Ask what?”

“Why are you so kind to me?”

“Don’t ask. It’s a question you’ll have to ask again soon enough.”

“What?”

With an enigmatic smile, Geom Mugeuk took her hand and pulled her along.

“Come on. We have to go show you off.”

“To whom?”

“Who else? To the worst bastard in the world—the one who teased you by calling you an ugly heart.”

Ian beamed at his words.

* * *

I walked down the road with Ian.

The people split apart to either side as though water were parting. Every one of them stared at her, completely entranced. Men and women alike looked at her. Children stared, and so did the elderly. Monks gazed at her, as did Daoists. Even the dog that had been barking stopped and stared at her.

—Everyone is looking at you. How does it feel?

—It’s too burdensome.

—Enjoy it.

—How am I supposed to enjoy it when they’re all staring like that?

—Enjoy those gazes. Ah, I wish people would look at me like that, too.

—Please, take them all!

—You won’t say that once you get used to it.

It was no different when we arrived at Cheonhwa Pavilion. Everyone’s attention focused on her. Exclamations of admiration rang out from all around.

There were famous beauties among the courtesans, but comparing them with Ian was impossible from the start. They possessed the same kind of beauty, yet Ian carried an air of noble dignity. She was not wearing even a trace of makeup, but her face seemed to radiate light.

And so we entered the room where Geukak Soma and the Cheonhwa Pavilion Lord were staying.

Geukak Soma was lying with his head on the Cheonhwa Pavilion Lord’s lap.

“Fortunately, you’re still here.”

“We waited until you arrived. What kept you so busy?”

Geukak Soma glanced at me, then saw Ian beside me and was startled. He sat upright.

I felt that Ian’s beauty could be summed up in a single sentence.

She was beautiful enough to make even the reclining Geukak Soma sit up.

“Who is she?”

“My new heart.”

“What happened to your old one?”

“I threw it away.”

“Did you perhaps kill that ugly heart?”

Knowing that he was asking sincerely, I laughed.

“Yes. I killed it with my own hands.”

“How could you kill it?”

“Why? Surely you don’t want to see that ugly heart again?”

“No. I haven’t finished paying back the money for repairing the carriage.”

Ian beamed at his words. When she smiled, it felt as though the entire room brightened.

Then the Cheonhwa Pavilion Lord casually asked Geukak Soma,

“She’s prettier than I was when I was young, isn’t she?”

I hurriedly looked at Geukak Soma.

‘Careful! It’s a trap, Soma.’

Chapter 118: A Cheeky and Pretty Heart.
---------------------------------------
“You were far prettier.”

Geugak Soma lightly leapt over the trap. At his words, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru beamed.

“Please don’t say that. I was never that beautiful.”

“No. In my eyes, you were far better.”

Geugak Soma, a man who had weathered every hardship imaginable, had shown that even a sudden ambush could not shake him in the slightest.

Even so, Geugak Soma’s gaze remained fixed on Yi An. Yes, it would not be easy for anyone to tear their eyes away from a woman this beautiful.

Even then, Geugak Soma still had not recognized Yi An, who had entered with us.

The one who recognized Yi An was the Mistress of Cheonhwaru.

“Congratulations, Martial Artist Yi.”

As though she had known this would happen all along, she was calm.

“Thank you, Mistress.”

The moment he heard Yi An’s voice, Geugak Soma was startled.

His eyes widened as he asked,

“Surely not that ugly Heart? Surely?”

He must have been truly shocked, for the words “surely not” came out twice.

Yi An smiled and said,

“Yes. I am that Heart, ugly though I was, with the amusing talent.”

Geugak Soma sprang to his feet and approached Yi An. He stared at her face from up close as though trying to bore through it. Under the pressure of the white mask, Yi An trembled in fear.

Geugak Soma tilted his head.

“There are some similarities, but no. It’s definitely not you.”

Geugak Soma looked at me.

“Are you trying to make fun of me?”

“I did come here to make fun of you, but she is also that Heart.”

“She is?”

Geugak Soma looked Yi An over again.

“That’s impossible. No, absolutely not!”

After denying it vehemently, he turned back to the Mistress of Cheonhwaru. With an expression that said it was true, she nodded once.

Geugak Soma asked me,

“What on earth happened?”

“This was her original appearance, but she had gained all that weight as a side effect of her protective art.”

“She looked like this to begin with, and she was working as a bodyguard? Isn’t that the greatest waste of talent in the history of our cult?”

At the same time, it was the greatest compliment Geugak Soma could give.

Yi An smiled and said to him,

“That was because I wanted to protect the young master.”

Of course, I was the one who corrected the facts.

“Do you think she did it because she wanted to? You know very well how our cult works.”

“Our cult works that ruthlessly, and that is why we serve as Demon Venerables and heirs. However…”

Geugak Soma’s gaze turned toward Yi An.

“This case will have to be treated as an exception.”

A new admiration entered Geugak Soma’s eyes as he looked at her. It was not admiration for her beauty, but for her sacrifice.

The Mistress of Cheonhwaru went to Yi An and took both her hands.

“You’re so beautiful. Congratulations.”

“Thank you.”

“Now, come with me. I’ll complete Martial Artist Yi for you.”

“Complete me?”

“You don’t know how to put on makeup, do you?”

“…No.”

Having lived her whole life as a guard warrior, when would she ever have had the chance to put on proper makeup?

“As you live, there will be times when you must fight with something other than a blade. For a woman, makeup is a weapon stronger than a sword.”

The Mistress of Cheonhwaru took her by the hand and led her out of the room. Yi An, too, seemed to want to learn about makeup, and allowed herself to be pulled along as if she had no choice.

“It’s stuffy in here. Let’s go out as well.”

“Very well.”

I left the room with Geugak Soma. The area around the Mistress of Cheonhwaru’s residence was beautifully tended, making it pleasant for a stroll.

“Being with the Second Young Master truly is one astonishing experience after another.”

“You must have been surprised. Even I, who resolved the side effect of her martial art, was surprised.”

But the astonishing experience Geugak Soma had just mentioned was not Yi An, but the battle from before.

“While we were fighting that day, I thought we might die at Baek Manggi’s hands.”

I had not known he would be that strong either.

But what was truly surprising was not Baek Manggi’s strength. It was the fact that Geugak Soma was now honestly revealing his feelings.

“I thought the same.”

“But you killed him, Second Young Master.”

“If Lord Soma had not helped me, I would have been the one to die.”

That was an undeniable fact.

Geugak Soma stared at me.

“I think I know what you’re thinking right now, Lord Soma.”

“What am I thinking?”

“So it really was that bastard who killed the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable!”

Geugak Soma burst into loud laughter.

“You’re wrong. I told you before, didn’t I? That I already believed it. I fought Baek Manggi because I believed it. If I hadn’t, I would have gotten out of there without looking back.”

“You believed in me? Even with your life on the line?”

After a brief pause, Geugak Soma answered.

“It seems I did.”

For a while, we walked there without speaking. The experience of crossing life and death together had given both of us great emotional turbulence. When Geugak Soma’s mask had almost been cut, I had truly been furious.

Only then did I realize for the first time that my feelings toward Geugak Soma were so strong. It seemed he felt the same.

Then, suddenly, Geugak Soma picked up a fallen branch from the ground and drew a long line.

“How far have we come now?”

I took the branch he held out and drew a line at the point we had once called the line of survival.

“I think we have come this far.”

It was the very point where he had said he would let me live if I made it this far.

At that moment, I saw it.

Through the eyeholes of the white mask, I saw him smiling. I saw him truly smile.

He laughed without making a sound, and this was not a laugh born of anger. Purely and naturally, he simply smiled.

I had never seen that smile even in my life before the regression, yet astonishingly, I saw it here at the end of this journey.

“Of course, depending on what you and I do from now on, the line will move. It may go farther this way, or it may return to the starting point.”

“What happens if it reaches the very end?”

“We’ll become true friends. We’ll eat together, drink together. We’ll enjoy things together, fight together. If one of us falls into danger, the other will go to save him. And if one of us dies first, the other will pour wine by his grave.”

“All of that is unnecessary, hypocritical, and burdensome.”

“Once you actually do it, they turn out to be no great things at all.”

I did not expect that much from Geugak Soma. My original goal had been to reach a point where we were neither enemies nor allies. At the very least, through this journey, I felt I had achieved that goal sufficiently.

Just then, Cheongmyeon appeared where we were.

He handed what he had brought to Geugak Soma. It was a white mask.

“Here, as promised.”

Geugak Soma gave me the mask Cheongmyeon had brought.

On the day we fought Baek Manggi, he had said he would give me a new mask, and he had not forgotten that promise.

“Thank you.”

I tried on the new mask. It was definitely lighter and more comfortable.

“This is nice. I don’t think it’ll get as sweaty, either.”

“Cheongmyeon made it himself. My mask was also made by Cheongmyeon.”

“You’re remarkably skilled with your hands.”

At my praise, Cheongmyeon bowed his head lightly.

Just then, two people walked in.

They were Yi An and the Mistress of Cheonhwaru, who had gone to put on makeup. Not only I, but even Geugak Soma and Cheongmyeon were startled.

Yi An with makeup looked like a completely different woman. She was enchanting and sensual. Intense and splendid.

“Martial Artist Yi already had such dignity, so I tried creating a different atmosphere.”

It truly felt as though we were looking at another peerless beauty. Makeup was a woman’s weapon? If so, we were beholding the Number One Sword Under Heaven.

“From now on, I’ll call you Pretty Heart instead of Ugly Heart.”

It seemed Geugak Soma was secretly pleased with Yi An’s transformation as well.

At that moment, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru casually said,

“She’s prettier than I was when I was young, isn’t she?”

At that, Geugak Soma said coldly to Yi An,

“Make sure you pay back the carriage fare!”

Meanwhile, after realizing from their conversation that the beauty before him was Yi An, Cheongmyeon was truly shocked.

“This person is Martial Artist Yi?”

Yi An smiled at the astonished man and answered,

“Yes, Cheongmyeon. It’s me.”

“You really are Martial Artist Yi?”

He was so surprised that I could imagine the expression beneath his mask. He was usually extremely careful in his behavior and sparing with his words in front of Geugak Soma, but he had even forgotten that he was standing before him.

Belatedly realizing that fact, Cheongmyeon stepped back.

Then Yi An cautiously said to me,

“May I wear that mask?”

Considering the gazes she had endured on the way here, I could understand Yi An. She was not the type to enjoy such looks.

“I don’t mind, but you should ask Lord Soma for permission. He’s the one who gave it to me.”

Yi An said to Geugak Soma,

“May I wear that mask?”

“No!”

Then we wouldn’t be able to see that pretty face, would we? I was certain that was what he was thinking.

“Why not?”

“The makeup she put so much effort into will be ruined. Do you know who did that makeup for you?”

Geugak Soma, who had given only the correct answers until the very end. The Mistress of Cheonhwaru happily pinched Geugak Soma’s arm. She was probably the only person in the world who could pinch him.

“If you want it, wear it.”

“Thank you.”

At Geugak Soma’s permission, Yi An was greatly delighted. She took the mask from me and put it on her face.

She looked at me and asked,

“How is it?”

“How is it? This is deception against beauty, and despair for all men under heaven.”

“You told me I should be happy, didn’t you? I think I’ll be happier with this.”

I went over and lifted Yi An’s mask up onto the top of her head.

“When it’s just us, wear it comfortably like this. At times like that, your mask will be looking up at the sky.”

“I like that.”

Perhaps because she had already begun, Yi An asked Cheongmyeon,

“Would a beauty trap work on you?”

Cheongmyeon did not understand what she meant and could not answer for a moment. I could guess what she was trying to say.

“If it’s you, it would work on anyone. You’d make even a monk who came down after a hundred years of wall-gazing break his vows.”

“You’re exaggerating too much!”

“Just wait and see later whether I am or not.”

Yi An said to Cheongmyeon again,

“What I said just now was a joke. I would like to invite you, Cheongmyeon, to become the first squad leader of the organization I will create.”

At the unexpected words, Cheongmyeon was startled. As he hesitated, not knowing how to answer, Geugak Soma spoke.

“I went to the trouble of giving you a mask as a gift, and the first thing you do while wearing it is try to steal my person away.”

“Please don’t misunderstand. Even before I received the mask, Cheongmyeon was my first candidate.”

“You’re telling him to give up the position of Demon Venerable and come be a squad leader. Do you understand that?”

“Yes!”

“Does Cheongmyeon look like that much of a fool to you?”

“No. I thought he was someone with the courage to throw himself into a new life at least once.”

“When it comes to courage, you impudent Heart, you are the greatest! Saying such things in front of me.”

“I blurted it out because I trusted the young master. I apologize.”

She bowed respectfully to Geugak Soma.

I saw it.

The conflict dwelling in Cheongmyeon’s eyes. Yes, if such a beautiful leader asked, any man would be conflicted.

But I also thought that this beauty might get in the way. The Yi An before the procedure might have convinced him to go, but there would also be those who would not want to look like shallow men going simply because she had become beautiful.

“Please think it over at least once.”

Cheongmyeon gave a slight nod.

Even after seeing that, Geugak Soma added nothing in particular. Even if Cheongmyeon said he would go, he looked like the sort of person who would simply say, “Idiot,” once and send him off. If he truly showed such a side of himself, the line drawn in my heart would move farther to the right.

Geugak Soma said to me,

“Second Young Master. Let us return now.”

“Please go ahead. I’ll play with Pretty Heart for a little while before coming back.”

It was not for my sake, but for Yi An’s. She had shed her old skin and emerged; she should soar freely for a while before returning.

The Mistress of Cheonhwaru saw through my thoughts precisely.

“Second Young Master, you truly are a good person.”

At that, Geugak Soma replied,

“Don’t be fooled. The Second Young Master is the villain among villains. Can’t you see that mask? He is a man who was born wearing one. Right now, there are two people wearing masks standing over there.”

The Mistress of Cheonhwaru smiled and said,

“That is why they suit each other.”

As I was about to turn and leave with Yi An, I turned back to Geugak Soma once more.

“Thank you so much for showing me such a wonderful world. Please show it to me again next time.”

Before we parted, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru said,

“Second Young Master, please take good care of my elder brother from now on.”

Ordinarily, it would have been a situation where Yi An should have said, Please take good care of our young master. But the Mistress of Cheonhwaru was playfully, yet earnestly, entrusting Geugak Soma to me.

Had she seen something in the relationship between Geugak Soma and me?

At least one thing was certain. In this world, only one person, the Mistress of Cheonhwaru, truly cared for him and understood him.

I laughed and said loudly,

“Yes. For your sake, Mistress, I’ll take good care of Lord Soma from now on.”

Leaving behind the dumbfounded Geugak Soma, I took Yi An by the hand and turned away.

“Let’s go, Yi An. To see the Central Plains.”

From now on, the world that would unfold before you would be completely different from the one you had known until now.

Chapter 119: Enjoy It, Ian!
---------------------------
“Can you feel all those eyes on you?”

Ian and I were at an inn. People had gathered and seated themselves around her. The seats on the opposite side were practically empty, yet everyone had clustered on our side.

They had all been inching closer, trying to get even the slightest better look at her face.

I whispered to Ian.

“That man on the left has looked at you six times, and that bastard on the right has looked seven times while pretending not to. The fellow beside him is openly staring, completely entranced. Hey, hey, stop looking! Pay attention to the young lady next to you! Ouch. That had to hurt.”

Just then, another customer came in. The man, looking around for a place to sit, widened his eyes when he saw Ian. As if bewitched, he walked toward her while staring, then tripped over a table and fell flat.

No one paid him any mind, and the fallen man sprang right back up. Then he took a seat where he could see Ian as well as possible. The man beside him was pouring wine, not even realizing the cup was overflowing.

“How does it feel?”

“It’s nice.”

“Nice? You’re not embarrassed?”

“I’m sure you made me sit here without my mask because you wanted to tease me when I got embarrassed, but your plan failed. I like this very, very much.”

“That’s unexpected.”

“I told you, didn’t I? I have more than one hidden tail. I can pretend to be embarrassed as much as I like, but I can also enjoy a few gazes like this as much as I like.”

Ian gave a faint smile toward the people watching her. Gasps erupted here and there.

She whispered.

“And that man on the left didn’t look six times. He looked eight.”

This is why I like Ian. She is flexible. She knows how to reveal her intelligence, and she knows how to hide it.

Even while carrying the side effects of the Whole-Body Petrification Art for her entire life, she had always lived with her head held high. She had wounds, and she had pride.

“Now then, we’ve enjoyed this inn enough. Let’s go buy you a veil.”

“A veil?”

“You can’t wear a mask all the time, can you? Use that mask whenever it’s really needed, and wear a veil normally.”

“I was planning to do that too. Saved myself some money.”

“Buy it with your own money!”

“Aww, buy it for me!”

At her “Aww,” several people collapsed, and at her “buy it for me,” several others clutched their money pouches.

When she and I stood up, disappointment and sighs swept through the surroundings. Looks of envy and jealousy flew fiercely toward me. Among them were also reproachful gazes telling me to buy her the veil.

She and I left the inn and walked together. The white mask dangled at her waist.

“From now on, there will be those who openly covet your beauty. All kinds of bastards will appear. Some will try to take you by force, some will try to drug you. There’ll be every sort of scum.”

I admit it. Whether it was intentional or unconscious, this was why I had postponed performing the Divine Poison Purification Art on her. Because I did not want to worry.

That did not mean I was no longer worried about her now that I had performed it. I would shake off my worry in a different way.

“You need the strength to protect yourself from such things. I intend to raise you to a level where you can face the Demon Venerables head-on.”

I had already been thinking that when I performed the Divine Poison Purification Art on her.

Ian did not offer needless humility. After all, she was not being taught just any martial art, but the Flying Heaven Sword Art, and with it came the natural goal of surpassing the Demon Venerables.

“I’ll work harder so I don’t cause you concern.”

“Don’t work any harder. You’re already overflowing with effort.”

She smiled, and only the old bachelor clerk at the fruit shop, who had been staring blankly, had his heart set aflutter.

We stopped by a cloth shop, and I bought her a bamboo hat with a veil attached. It had the advantage of completely hiding her face, but after trying it on, she refused.

“This is too uncomfortable. First of all, my field of vision is too narrow, so I think I’d be too vulnerable to a surprise attack. I’ll only be able to wear this after I become more skilled.”

“Then let’s just buy a veil for now.”

I bought her a veil that covered below the eyes like a face covering.

The veil alone could not hide her beauty, but it was still a little better.

“When I make the Ghost Shadow Unit later, I’m going to make their masks truly stylish.”

Imagining her wearing a mask with a distinctive ghost pattern, I thought she would look truly mysterious and splendid. Though if Blue Mask saw that sight, I had a feeling he might not be able to endure it.

Whenever she found a place where she could see her reflection, she stopped walking.

She was simply not expressing it all, but how happy must she be right now?

Enjoy it to the fullest, Ian!

“Where are we going now?”

“Can you climb mountains?”

“Mountains?”

“Your body’s lighter now, so let’s climb a mountain. Come on, run.”

After running for quite a while using qinggong, we arrived below a cliff of strange, jagged rock.

“Step only where I step!”

As she followed me up the cliff using qinggong, she could not utter a single word. It was her first time climbing such a sheer cliff.

Only after we reached the top did she let out a sigh of relief.

“To be honest, I was terrified on the way up.”

Her swordsmanship had reached a high level, but her qinggong did not match that skill.

“If you fall, you die, right?”

“You have to not die to be called an expert. With your swordsmanship, you ought not to die.”

“If I do fall, how should I respond?”

“Keep your wits about you and slow your fall by any means you can.”

Of course, in my case, when I was almost at the ground, I would release my internal energy and land lightly, but that was beyond Ian’s current ability. It would consume a great deal of internal energy, but she would have to slow herself as much as possible from the top as she fell.

“You know this too, but it’s hard to survive with swordsmanship alone. You need to be good at qinggong, have courage, possess sound judgment, and be able to read people.”

“Ah! It was warm inside the greenhouse.”

It was her joke about having lived comfortably under the protection of me and our cult, but I do not think so.

Not once had she ever been inside a greenhouse. With the emotional scars caused by the side effects of her martial art, and with her barely sleeping because she was guarding me—what kind of greenhouse is that harsh? After I regressed, she had barely slept because of training.

“Outside the greenhouse, is it spring? Or winter?”

“The jianghu has never once been spring.”

“Like this frightening cliff.”

She looked down below the cliff. I spoke as if whispering into her ear.

“Do you remember what I told you earlier? What to do when you fall.”

“Of course. Keep my wits about me and slow my fall.”

“Then that’s enough.”

The next instant, I shoved her off the cliff.

“Aaaaaaah!”

With a desperate scream, she fell.

I leapt down after her.

I thought she would fall in a complete mess, but Ian was flailing as she struggled to regain her balance and somehow slow her descent.

However, she still lacked experience, so her body was far too tense, and she was needlessly consuming internal energy.

I instantly increased my speed and caught her in midair.

Only then did Ian, held in my arms, relax.

“Cling to me.”

“Yes.”

Then, as if to let her see, I showed her how to slow down using my feet and arms. Even while holding her, our speed decreased in an instant.

Clinging to me, Ian watched without missing a single movement.

As we fell like that, I threw myself toward the cliff. There was a small cave halfway down.

Only after we landed there did Ian finally shout.

“I really thought I was going to die!”

“Liar. You trusted me to save you, didn’t you?”

“Well, yes, but still. I was really shocked. The speed of the fall was much faster than I expected, so I couldn’t get my balance.”

“If you keep falling, you’ll get used to it, and gradually you’ll be able to find your balance.”

“Yes. I remember well how you did it.”

“Then that’s enough.”

“But I had no idea there was a cave in a place like this.”

“Remember its location. If you’re ever chased in this area, take refuge here.”

“How did you know about this place?”

“Do you think this cave is the only thing I know?”

“Of course not. That’s why I’m not going to ask anymore. But why did you come here?”

“From now on, I’m going to open your Ren and Du meridians.”

Because I had said it without setting any mood for an important conversation, at first she only blinked. Then her eyes gradually widened in surprise, and the final size of that surprise was even greater than when I said I would perform the Divine Poison Purification Art. At least I had given her some warning then. This time, it was something she had never even imagined.

Opening the Ren and Du meridians.

It was something one had to undergo in order to become a true expert, but it was also the greatest fortuitous encounter that only an extremely small number of martial artists could experience.

This process was incredibly dangerous and difficult, so there were more than a few who had died trying to recklessly open the Ren and Du meridians. Moreover, there were also cases where the person performing the opening died from qi and blood flowing in reverse.

There was no need to look far for examples. As blood kin of the Heavenly Demon, we had our Ren and Du meridians opened when we were young, and I still vividly remember how much my elder brother frightened me when it was my turn. He must have hoped I would fail to overcome my fear and suffer qi deviation, but I succeeded in opening my Ren and Du meridians safely.

In any case, the opening of the Ren and Du meridians was not something one should either perform or receive carelessly. That was why the old masters of prestigious orthodox sects risked danger only for the legitimate successors of their sects.

“You’re really going to open my Ren and Du meridians?”

“Yes.”

“Why on earth are you so good to me… Ah, so that’s why you told me not to ask. Because it was a question I would have to ask again soon.”

After I finished the Divine Poison Purification Art, I had told her not to ask.

“It’s a gift. A gift celebrating your new life.”

Ian could not say anything.

Once again, tears welled in her eyes.

“I hate crying, but Young Master keeps making me cry.”

“This isn’t something to cry over.”

“You’re right. I’m happy. I’m truly happy enough to feel like I could fly.”

“Now then, shall we begin? Since something might go wrong, leave your final words.”

“Please tell my young master. Tell him I’m sorry I couldn’t serve him to the end.”

“I’ll scold that young master of yours. He’s monopolizing you far too much.”

“He won’t be easy to scold. He’s growing stronger with each passing day.”

At her words, I ended up laughing.

“Now, let’s start with qi circulation.”

She sat cross-legged and began operating her mental cultivation method. When her true qi completed one heavenly cycle, I infused a mighty stream of internal energy into her back.

Slowly, I moved my internal energy along with hers through her meridians.

“From now on, you don’t need to answer me. No matter what happens, you must not scream or stop circulating your qi. You must not lose consciousness either.”

As she circulated her qi and steadied her mind, the speed of my internal force moving along her meridians gradually increased.

The Ren meridian begins at the lips, descends along the chest and abdomen, and reaches the Huiyin point. The Du meridian begins at the Huiyin point, rises along the tailbone and spine, passes through the Baihui point, and then continues back to the lips. The most important moment here was the instant of piercing through the Baihui point. If anything went wrong with the Baihui point at the crown of the head, one could be crippled or lose one’s life.

I knew well that finishing it in a single instant was better than dragging it out. My pure and profound internal energy boldly pushed through her meridians.

There was nothing to worry about. This was an opening performed by none other than me.

Chengjiang, Lianquan, Xuanji, Yutang, Zhongwan… I pierced through the meridians with unstoppable momentum. Like all kinds of filth being swept away by torrential rain, the impurities blocking her blood vessels began to break apart.

Ian suffered from the unfamiliar pain, but she was no stranger to enduring agony.

I was so unhesitating that one might wonder if it was all right to pierce through so forcefully. In truth, accidents happened when one hesitated. If it did not open in one go, you had to circle back and come again, and then again, striking fiercely.

Yangguan, Lingdao, Shenzhu, Yamen, Naohu, Houding. After opening all the other meridians like that, we reached the critical juncture.

‘Trust me, Ian.’

‘I trust you.’

‘Now!’

Rumble-rumble-rumble! Boom!

Even the most difficult Baihui point was cleanly pierced through, and as the remaining meridians were all opened, her Ren and Du meridians were completely unblocked.

As Ian slowly collapsed to the side, a smile rested on her lips.

‘Well done, Ian.’

Ian woke only after sleeping for half a day.

“Young Master? What happened?”

“Now, circulate your qi.”

With a tense expression, Ian sat down and began circulating her qi. After completing one heavenly cycle, her face brightened radiantly.

“It’s different. It’s completely different from before. My internal energy is much purer, and the movement of qi is unobstructed.”

“Congratulations, Ian.”

Ian sprang up and tried to bow to me, but I released internal force and stopped her.

“I told you before, didn’t I? I did it so I could work you hard. There’s no need to be grateful.”

“Just how hard do you plan to work me? This is enough gratitude that even if you told me to unify the entire murim, I’d have to do it.”

“Let’s eat first before unifying the murim. You’re hungry, aren’t you?”

“Hungry enough to go raid the Murim Alliance kitchen.”

Standing in front of the cave, she shouted.

“I opened my Ren and Du meridians—aaaaaah!”

From behind, I pushed Ian again. Watching her fall with less flailing than before, I also leapt down from the cliff.

One double hour later, Ian and I were eating again at an inn in the marketplace.

We ordered all sorts of delicious dishes and ate together. We paid no attention to the gazes of the other customers.

“It’s really delicious.”

“Let’s travel around and eat every famous dish in the Central Plains.”

“What if I get fat again?”

“Even if you want to, you won’t be able to. Once we return to our cult, you’ll fall into a hell of training until you achieve mastery of the Flying Heaven Sword Art.”

“Ah, let’s never go back!”

She was saying things she did not mean, but she was enjoying this moment so very much.

Just then, a young man came walking toward us, pushing his way through the crowd.

He was rather handsome and wore expensive, splendid clothes, and behind him were as many as four martial artists who appeared to be his guards.

Ian whispered as if chanting a spell.

“Don’t come, don’t come. Let us just eat in peace.”

Watching her, I smiled.

These are things you will experience countless times from now on. Now, how will you deal with it?

The man came before Ian and spoke politely with a confident expression.

“Young lady, may I treat you to a cup of wine?”

Now then, Ian. This is where it begins.

Chapter 120: Not the Cheonsa Sect, the Cheonyang Sect.
------------------------------------------------------
Ian sent me a voice transmission.

—What should I do?

—He’s offering to buy you a drink, so handle it yourself.

I intended to watch how she dealt with it.

—This is the first time in my life this has ever happened.

—It’s my first time watching it happen from beside you, too.

—I can really do as I please?

—Do it.

—I really will.

The Feitian Sword Art was fine, and opening the Ren and Du meridians was fine, but the most important weapon that would protect her was experience.

The man introduced himself.

“I am Gu Si (穀思), young sect master of the Tianrangmen (天壤門).”

He was dressed in expensive silk, his wrists and fingers adorned with all manner of costly jewels. Even his fan was set with dazzling gems.

“Have you perhaps heard of our sect?”

“No. My experience is limited, so I’m afraid I haven’t. I’m sorry.”

“Not at all. That can happen.”

In truth, the Tianrangmen was a fairly well-known sect in the area.

Judging by his attire alone, Gu Si was the sort to go around declaring, “Do you know what family I’m from?” So the fact that she did not know him meant he had lost his greatest weapon.

“And what sect might you belong to, young lady?”

“It’s an unnamed sect far out in the countryside, so you wouldn’t know it.”

“Then at least tell me your name.”

“Baekmyeon.”

She answered that way because she had thought of a white mask. Gu Si was slightly taken aback by the strange name Baekmyeon, but soon smiled and said,

“Now then, Miss Baekmyeon. Shall we move somewhere else and talk?”

“That would be a little difficult. We’re in the middle of a conversation ourselves.”

Only then did Gu Si look at me. Truly, ever since sitting down, he had not spared me a single glance.

“And who are you, young hero?”

While Ian hesitated over how to introduce me, I answered first.

“I am Miss Baekmyeon’s guard.”

For an instant, Gu Si’s expression creased slightly. It meant, Why is a guard sharing a table with her? And with that single reaction, he revealed the kind of person he was.

I sent Ian a voice transmission.

—For today, I’ll be your guard. You spent your whole life as my guard, so one day the other way around is fine, isn’t it? Enjoy it. Chances like this don’t come often.

—I really am going to enjoy it.

Gu Si said to Ian,

“You seem close to your guard.”

Looking at me, Ian spoke with eyes full of mischief.

“No matter how many times I tell him to live his own life, he refuses to leave my side.”

Gu Si glared at me. The look in his eyes said exactly this: Your mistress is beautiful, so you’re clinging to her and trying to pull something, aren’t you?

“I wonder what you could possibly have to discuss with a guard.”

“We talk about all sorts of things.”

“If I may offer one piece of advice, it is better to keep your distance from guards.”

Ian answered Gu Si’s words.

“Building affection with a guard means building up points so they’ll die in your place, right?”

I laughed inwardly. Right now, she was throwing back all the things I had once said to her.

With an expression that seemed to say, What nonsense is that? Gu Si replied,

“That is not why.”

“Then why?”

“When you give subordinates an opening, they are bound to harbor other thoughts. It is not good for them either.”

He wore a solemn expression, but dressed in all those dangling jewels, what life experience could he possibly be speaking from? I could feel that it was mere posturing to look impressive in front of a woman.

“How cute.”

“Pardon? Ah, yes. There are times when subordinates who harbor other thoughts can seem cute.”

“No, I mean you’re cute, Young Hero Gu.”

For a moment, Gu Si was flustered. He had not expected her to say so openly that he was cute.

“Very well. Buy me a drink. Take me to the most expensive tavern in this town and buy me the most expensive liquor there.”

Ian rose from her seat.

“What is it? Did you change your mind because I asked for expensive liquor?”

“Of course not. Let us go.”

We left the tavern. Ian was acting differently from what I had expected. I had not thought she would so readily accept an invitation to drink.

A short while later, we took seats in a private room at the most expensive tavern in town.

“Young Hero Gu. Since we’re going to have fun anyway, call all your friends too. Men, women, call them all and let’s enjoy ourselves together. What’s wrong? You don’t have any friends?”

Gu Si’s face flushed slightly.

“In the martial world, the only one you can trust is yourself.”

It seemed he had been taught that since childhood. Trust no one.

“So you don’t have any friends. Then I’ll have to be our lonely Young Hero Gu’s friend.”

With her unique cheerfulness, Ian was guiding what could have been an endlessly depressing situation. The atmosphere grew infinitely brighter, and she was sincerely trying to enjoy herself. It felt exactly like this.

You want to drink? Fine. Why wouldn’t I? Since we’re having fun anyway, let’s really have fun!

In the meantime, a sumptuous feast was laid out. As one could tell from his attire, having nothing but money was his exclusive martial art.

“This liquor costs more than fifty nyang a bottle.”

At Gu Si’s boast, Ian made a great fuss.

“It’s my first time drinking something this expensive! Now then, I’ll drink gratefully. Ah, I’ll give my guard a cup as well.”

Without waiting for his answer, Ian poured me a drink.

“Guard Geom, accept a cup. This is very expensive liquor, so don’t leave a drop.”

“Before that, I will inspect it for a moment.”

I took out a silver needle and tested the liquor and dishes one by one. Of course, Gu Si made all sorts of faces, but Ian smiled and said to him,

“Young Hero Gu, surely you wouldn’t do anything strange to the food, would you? If you did, you’d be in trouble! Truly in trouble.”

“What do you take me for? Even without doing such things, women line up for me.”

At that, Ian smiled sweetly. It was the kind of captivating smile that could even melt an enemy’s heart.

“I knew it. Now, now, let’s drink.”

The three of us raised our cups. After emptying her cup, Ian expressed her regret.

“It’s a shame for only us to drink such fine liquor. Guards outside!”

This time, she called in Gu Si’s guards waiting outside. Four guards entered the room.

“Young Hero Gu, shall we drink with them too?”

Immediately, Gu Si looked displeased.

One of the four guards stepped forward.

“We are fine. We must protect the young master.”

Ian said to him,

“Is your young master truly protected while you stand out there?”

At those meaningful words, the guard had nothing to say. Until now, all they had been doing was guarding the drinking parties of a wastrel who went around seducing women with money.

Soon, Ian gently persuaded Gu Si.

“Young Hero Gu, don’t attach too much meaning to this. We’re simply having a drink together. Come now, sit.”

The guards watched Gu Si’s expression.

When Ian looked slightly disappointed, Gu Si could not hold out any longer. Once Gu Si nodded, the four men sat down.

“Now, now, each of you accept a cup from me.”

She poured them drinks. Perhaps Ian felt a tender sympathy toward them. They had been guards like her.

“Now, let’s all drink together!”

She raised her cup high, and we toasted.

“Ah, the liquor tastes good. Young Hero Gu, order more! Ah, is it too expensive?”

“What are you saying? Order as much as you like.”

Beauty was overwhelming everything.

And when Ian’s unique cheerfulness and vitality were added to it, nothing could stop her.

I truly had never imagined such an atmosphere could be created with these people.

She was unreserved. Because she held no prejudice toward the other party, no one felt emotionally uncomfortable.

I could tell it was not simply because I was beside her.

Once again, it was because of her confidence in her ability. Opening the Ren and Du meridians must have given even greater strength to her self-assurance.

We drank and talked about all sorts of things. The guards, who at first were extremely sparing with their words, began answering questions one by one, and even things Gu Si had not known came to light.

“You had a son ten days ago? Oh, congratulations!”

Ian did not interrogate Gu Si, who naturally would not have known. Instead, she created an atmosphere for everyone.

“Now, a gift for our young master! A gift! A gift!”

I admired her. She allowed them to celebrate without putting Gu Si in a bad mood. To be honest, at that moment, I thought this: She’s better than me.

Gu Si had never once given his guards a gift before, so he did not know what to do.

Ian calmly said to him,

“Among the people I know, there are some who like drawing lines.”

She picked up a chopstick, dipped it in liquor, and drew a long line across the table.

“This is the moment they’re hired, and this is the end. If they make it all the way here, they may even die in their employer’s place. Making it to the end? That is truly not an easy thing. And yet guards hope for it. They hope the relationship between themselves and the person they protect will reach this end. Even though this is the line where they die. That is what the guards here are.”

At her calm words, the four guards became slightly stirred. Those words were also meant for me. That she herself was like that.

“You know a great deal about guards.”

The question came from one of the guards.

“I experienced it when I was young.”

At that moment, Gu Si glared at the guard. His eyes said, Who told you to speak up? The guard who had stepped forward shrank back and lowered his head.

Ian said gently to Gu Si,

“If you look at people with those eyes, the line goes the other way.”

“I do not believe it. That someone can purely devote their life to someone else. Do you believe it, young lady?”

Ian nodded and held out her cup.

“Young Hero Gu should try believing it once too.”

“If you believe, you’ll get stabbed in the back properly.”

“Do you think you won’t if you don’t believe?”

“!”

“We often connect betrayal with trust. But that is only the position of the one being betrayed. I trusted them this much, and yet they betrayed me! But from the betrayer’s side, it’s not as though they betray because the other person didn’t trust them, is it? Most of the time, it’s for some other reason, isn’t it? Money, power, their own personal affairs. I have never once seen a case where someone said, My master did not trust me, so I betrayed them. That is an excuse made afterward. They betrayed because they weren’t trusted. So what I’m saying is, you can believe! Betrayal is a separate matter anyway!”

“That is sophistry!”

“Let us drink to today’s sophistry!”

Gu Si could not refuse the cup Ian offered him.

He was drunk, but Ian was only pretending to be drunk.

I saw it. From time to time, she was expelling the liquor’s energy through the tips of her fingers. Even though she seemed to be playing wildly, she was being careful. Perfect, Ian!

—When did you learn that? How to expel liquor energy?

—I learned when I was young. I simply lacked the ability to do it until now. Ah, what a waste. This expensive liquor!

As if she had belatedly remembered, she shouted,

“Ah, right. The baby’s gift! A gift! Give him a gift!”

When she shouted in high spirits, Gu Si took a money pouch from his robes.

“A lot! A lot! A lot!”

In the end, a fairly large sum came out of the pouch.

“It’s all right.”

“Take it. Buy the child some clothes or something.”

“Thank you.”

Looking at him, Ian gave him a thumbs-up.

“Young Hero Gu! You’re wonderful!”

“It is nothing.”

For no reason, Gu Si’s face turned red.

Thanks to that, the atmosphere grew even better, and we drank all night. Though we had only met that day, we shared all kinds of stories. It was also the first time Gu Si and his guards had ever shared a drinking party.

With Ian leading the mood, everyone gradually became soaked in the enjoyment of the drinking party, like getting wet in a drizzle. We drank through the night, talking about every trivial thing. We talked about martial arts, about making money, and about our families.

Because Ian, who led the gathering, treated the others sincerely, everyone unconsciously revealed their sincerity as well. We drank so merrily that we were genuinely sorry to part.

“Young Hero Gu, next time I come to this town, I’ll look for the Cheonsa Sect first.”

“It is not the Cheonsa Sect, but the Tianrangmen.”

“Ah, ah, our Tianrangmen’s Young Hero Gu. I’ve definitely remembered. Young Hero Gu, from today on, we’re drinking friends.”

At the words “from today on, we’re drinking friends,” Gu Si could not hide his joy. Perhaps when he first approached her today, he had begun with the thought of somehow getting Ian into bed. But now, he was moved by the words “drinking friends.”

“And until we meet again, you mustn’t go around seducing just any woman! Train in martial arts too. Let’s meet again when you look impressive. Understood?”

“Understood.”

Amazingly, Gu Si was nodding. I did not know for certain, but it felt as if he would change from this day onward. He even gave me some spending money.

“Serve the young lady well.”

“Yes, thank you.”

Ah, Gu Si would never know. How much I was admiring her at this very moment. Not admiring Gu Si, but Ian. In the span of a single night, she had made five men fall completely under her charm. To be honest, it was six. Because I, too, truly admired her.

And so we left that place. Far behind us, Gu Si and his guards watched us go, reluctant to say farewell.

“To be honest, at first I was worried about you.”

“About me? Why?”

“I was afraid you’d be deceived and swayed by men. Afraid you’d get worked up and cause trouble. Afraid you’d be disappointed by men and hurt.”

When they first appeared, I had thought it would end with Gu Si getting beaten up, or making even more of a fuss and dying. Something like that.

But it ended in a way I had never expected at all. If we had dragged Gu Si to Huangquan Pavilion, frightened him, and beaten him, would he have changed like that? It felt as though sunlight had removed the outer garment the wind could not strip away.

“And?”

“Now I have the complete opposite worry.”

“What kind of worry?”

“I’m worried about the men. If I let you out, you’ll ruin all the men of the martial world. They’ll squander their fortunes, go mad with training to show you how strong they are, and fall lovesick.”

Ian burst into laughter.

“You know, don’t you? How many tails I have.”

After seeing her today, I thought I absolutely had to help her master the Feitian Sword Art. Because the stronger she became, the more charming a person she would become.

“Where are we going now?”

“If we meet someone like Gu Si, we’ll have fun together again, eat delicious things, admire the scenic views along the way. And we’ll go watch a wedding.”

“Whose wedding?”

“There is one. A wedding I’m debating whether to stop or not.”

“Why would you stop someone else’s wedding?”

“Right?”

“That sounds fun! I’ve never properly watched a wedding before!”

Excited, she ran ahead.

Thinking that the sight of her running toward the sun rising in the distance was like a painting, I too slowly seeped into that painting.

Chapter 121: How Easily One Forgets.
------------------------------------
Ian and I continued our journey.

Our destination was the Ghost Gate, the clan of the Ghost Spirit. There was still plenty of time before the wedding would take place, so I was traveling with Ian, enjoying the trip and keeping to a leisurely schedule.

Along the way, we met many people. We encountered lecherous bastards who coveted her, and a naive young noble who claimed he adored her so truly he would lay down his life. There was even a wealthy old man who brought a bundle of gold to propose. The lechers died, the noble wept, and the old man was humiliated.

Meeting various people was a fresh and pleasant experience, but unexpectedly, what she enjoyed even more was taking in the scenic sights and tasting the delicious local cuisine. People might be tiresomely repetitive, but to her, the Central Plains—which she was seeing for the first time—must have been wondrous at every turn.

She endeavored to remember each moment as though she would never come here again. She even wrote down the things that happened during the journey.

“This dish is really delicious. Young Master, you should try it too.”

“I prefer this one.”

“But that one is so spicy. If you like spicy food too much, you’ll ruin your stomach.”

“You wouldn’t survive in Sichuan.”

“Even so, I want to try proper Sichuan cuisine.”

By now, we paid no mind to the stares of those around us. People still stole glances at her beauty, but since we didn’t care, they became nonexistent.

“But Young Master. Is it really okay for us to be playing around like this?”

“Why wouldn’t it be?”

“The First Young Master is probably working hard right now to bring the Demon Lords to his side.”

“No doubt.”

“What if the Blood Heaven Demon joins hands with the First Young Master again?”

“Please, tell him to do exactly that. How entertaining would the process of recruiting him again be?”

“You’re so carefree that it makes my heart uneasy.”

“Don’t be uneasy. If it is my destiny to become the successor, I can return slowly. If it is not my destiny, I won’t succeed even if I meet Demon Lords every day. And besides, isn’t there something far more important I am doing right now than making Demon Lords my allies?”

“What is it?”

“Feeding the fish I’ve already caught.”

She pointed at herself with her finger, and I nodded.

How foolish one can be. How easily one forgets that without the fish already caught, there would be no chance to catch new ones. How readily one takes another person’s goodwill for granted. What in the world can compare to the gratitude of someone who let herself be caught first and continues to stay by your side?

She said with a smile.

“The fish has been eating well lately.”

“So don’t complain later! Saying there wasn’t enough affection, or not enough trust!”

“In that case, order me just one more of those!”

“Order everything next to it too!”

Of course, even while traveling, we did not neglect our martial arts training.

Running, leaping, and sparring. Truly, aside from the time spent eating and enjoying the scenery, we devoted every moment to martial arts training.

Especially these days, whenever I had a spare moment, I was pondering the essence of martial arts that the Lord of All Matters had left behind.

That enlightenment, mingling with the grand completion of the Soaring Heaven Sword Technique’s Twelve Stars, was somehow tickling at me. I knew this sensation always led to growth, so I did not neglect my training for even a moment.

Ian, for her part, was also immersed in martial arts training. Especially since her Governing and Conception meridians had been opened, she had reached the final stages of the Soaring Heaven Sword Technique’s Eight Stars. Now, with just a little more effort, she would reach the Nine Stars.

Alongside swordsmanship training, lightness skill training was indispensable.

“Aaaaaah!”

Ian, falling from the cliff, was still screaming, but her movements were different from before. Her flailing arms and legs moved with composure.

At the last moment, she twisted her body several times and landed on the ground. She staggered and fell forward, but she had succeeded in falling from the cliff on her own.

“I did it!”

She jumped up and down in joy.

I came down after her and landed by her side. In the final moments, I descended through the air as if walking down invisible stairs, and she was startled.

“I jumped after you so many times that I’ve improved a bit too.”

“Are you trying to crush my spirit like this? I came down after all that struggle, and you do this?!”

“I’m only human too; I want to show off and boast, don’t I?”

“Even without showing off, you’re impressive enough.”

“Ah, I want to keep acting impressive so I can keep hearing those words.”

“Go ahead. I’ll say them tirelessly for you.”

Devoted to me though she is, she is someone who loves to have fun, who is cheerful and bright, clever, and values her own life and happiness. That is why I am all the more grateful for her, and why I like her so.

Ian drew her sword.

“Now, you have to pay me a compliment fee. Please grant me a bout.”

I fought a fierce round with her.

We deliberately fought while flying up the cliff using lightness skill. Sword qi flew through the air, and we kicked off the cliff to soar upward while dodging the sword qi.

She felt the crisis of death several times. She fell from the cliff and climbed back up, and sword qi flew toward her. Narrowly dodging the sword qi, she unleashed her own sword qi and had to scale the cliff.

It was a fight between true experts. Though I set the stage and guided her, she responded magnificently. I could feel her skill growing. I, too, felt things while teaching her.

Thus, we ended the fight when we reached the top of the cliff again.

“After opening the Governing and Conception meridians, the flow of true qi and the power of the techniques have changed.”

“You know this is an important time, right?”

“I know.”

Her eyes were filled with resolve. Scenic views were nice, and delicious food was nice, but she was a born martial artist as well. Nothing could compare to the joy of the moment when one’s martial arts grew.

“You’re doing well.”

“With what master I have, how could I not?”

Her eyes looking at me were filled with gratitude and respect.

“You said you wanted to see the wedding, right? Beyond that mountain over there is the place we’re headed.”

“But why are you hesitating about whether to stop the wedding or not?”

“I was asked. To stop the wedding.”

“By whom?”

“There is someone. Someone who helped me greatly.”

I could explain everything else, but this was a difficult part to explain.

Truthfully, though I had coldly told him to get married and live a hellish life again, he weighed on my mind.

If it weren’t for the Ghost Spirit, I wouldn’t have been able to regress. I understood why he had wanted to betray me for his own goal.

An opportunity to return to the past—how could a human resist such temptation? Could it be that the reason he wanted to go back, even if it meant betraying me, was not to escape that marriage?

I would make the final decision after meeting him, but I would likely try to stop the marriage if I could. He had longed for it that desperately.

“Is this someone who loves the woman getting married?”

“I don’t think so?”

There was no enemy quite like that. After all, the Ghost Spirit had described his married life as hell.

“There is also someone I want to see, even if it weren’t for the wedding.”

Xu Jin.

My only friend from my days as a wanderer. If she—the Ghost Spirit’s younger sister—hadn’t told me about the Regression Grand Method, I would never have known.

“Now, let’s get going.”

I leaped from the cliff. From behind, I heard Ian’s cry.

“I told you I’m someone who’s scared of heights! You can’t just go like that!”

But contrary to her words, Ian, too, leaped after me without hesitation.

* * *

The night at the Ghost Gate was deep.

Xu Kong, the Ghost Spirit, had become incredibly busy after becoming the successor of the Ghost Gate, so much so that he lacked time to sleep.

During the day, he had to master the secret ghost techniques passed down only to the successor of the Ghost Gate, and at night, he had to research the Regression Grand Method. It had been the clan’s long-cherished wish for ages, with thousands of volumes of inherited materials alone.

Today, too, he was reading until late.

At that moment, a familiar voice came from behind.

“If you had devoted this much sincerity to learning swordsmanship, you would have become the greatest swordsman under heaven.”

The Ghost Spirit answered, keeping his eyes on his book.

“If you had used the time you spend meddling to learn swordsmanship, you would have become number one.”

The person who had entered the room was his younger sister, Xu Jin.

“If you overwork yourself like that, you’ll get sick. If you collapse, Brother, all that work falls on me.”

“Don’t worry. I made a promise with a ghost. That it wouldn’t take me until this research is finished.”

“The ghost I met didn’t say that?”

Only then did the Ghost Spirit take his eyes from the book and turn to look at her.

“Tsk, our brother’s ugly face has gotten even uglier because he’s tired.”

“Am I not okay, at my level?”

“Only the mirror entertains such delusions, Brother.”

The Ghost Spirit laughed loudly. Among the daily routine, his younger sister was the one who gave him a chance to breathe.

Xu Jin placed the documents she had brought on the desk.

“Here. I organized everything you told me to organize.”

“Good work.”

“It was tough. I’m not a genius like you, Brother. I have to read what you read once five times over. Why is it so needlessly difficult? It was really hard.”

“I know, I know.”

The Ghost Spirit felt sorry for his sister. She was spending her flowering youth solely on research.

Her looks were outstanding too. Above all, she had a refreshing personality. She would be popular if she went outside, but these days she was holed up in the research room.

Xu Jin brought up something she had never mentioned before.

“Brother, do you truly believe the Regression Grand Method is possible?”

“I believe.”

The Ghost Spirit answered without hesitation.

“Even though it defies the natural order?”

“Still, I believe.”

How could the Ghost Spirit not know why his sister was asking this?

“It’s hard, isn’t it?”

“It’s stifling. If we spend our time researching like this and pass it on to our children... won’t our children also be trapped in this research room for their whole lives?”

“I will definitely succeed in my generation.”

“If you’re going to do it, please finish it before I get much older. Otherwise....”

The Ghost Spirit felt it. His sister was nearing her limit. She had originally been an active person. Though she followed their father’s orders because she had no choice, no one knew how long she could endure.

Xu Jin, who had been about to turn around and leave, stopped in front of the door.

“Fine, let’s say the Regression Grand Method is possible, as you say. Then what? If you go to the past? If you go back to the past, what will you do, Brother? Will you lock yourself up again to do research? Since you’ve achieved regression, will you research reincarnation next?”

“!”

“If you thought of something else instead... Brother, aren’t we living the wrong lives? Shouldn’t we be doing that with our lives right now? The life you want to live—shouldn’t you be living it?”

The Ghost Spirit could not say a word.

There was much to say, but Xu Jin did not press her brother any further.

“Sleep early. You really look terrible.”

Once she left the research room, the Ghost Spirit leaned back in his chair with a sigh.

His sister was right. If he lived his life faithfully now, there would be no reason to go back. But achieving the Regression Grand Method was the family’s long-cherished wish. Now that he was the successor, it was an unavoidable fate.

At that moment, a servant arrived with welcome news. The person he had been waiting for so long would arrive here tomorrow.

* * *

The place Ian and I arrived at was an inn in the village where the Ghost Gate was located.

Ian wore a mask at her waist and had put on a veil, revealing only her eyes.

After securing a room with two beds, we ordered a meal.

When the two of us stayed at inns while traveling, we stayed in one room like this. Even when I told her to sleep separately, asking if it wasn’t uncomfortable, Ian actually preferred it. Her reason was that it was fun to fall asleep while talking about this and that.

And so we were eating on the first floor of the inn with Ian.

The guests around us were talking about the news of the Ghost Spirit’s wedding at the Ghost Gate. Lately, it seemed the wedding had indisputably been the talk of the town.

“Who would have thought the Ghost Gate would form an in-law relationship with the Lin Family Sword Gate.”

“I was surprised too. Hasn’t the Lin Family Sword Gate formed alliances with sword families generation after generation?”

“They say the young lady Lin persuaded her father.”

“The union of the Ghost Gate and a sword family—it’s bound to be interesting.”

“Ah, isn’t that the successor of the Ghost Gate over there?”

Ian’s and my gazes followed them toward the outside.

A man appeared on the roadside. Seeing him, my heart surged with emotion. He was the Ghost Spirit in his youth. Since I had seen him from middle age onward, all of his features from that time were still intact.

*It’s good to see you, Ghost Spirit.*

In my life before regression, I had only met him briefly a few times, but those encounters had spanned decades at the most crucial moments. Memories of the hardships I endured to procure the Grand Method ingredients on my own came flooding back.

The Ghost Spirit was looking across the road as if waiting for someone.

After a while, about five or six people arrived there on horseback. When the woman at the front dismounted, those following behind dismounted as well. Judging by appearances, they were martial artists escorting the woman.

The Ghost Spirit ran toward her.

“Miss Lin!”

She was none other than Lin Xiang, the Ghost Spirit’s fiancée.

“Young Master? Why are you here?”

“I came out to greet you upon hearing of your arrival.”

Honey dripped from the Ghost Spirit’s eyes as he looked at her.

“Thank you for accepting my proposal this time.”

“It is a decision I made because I trust you.”

“What of your father?”

“He has given his permission, but... I think he needs a bit more time.”

“I will surely win your father’s heart.”

The Ghost Spirit spoke loudly, paying no heed to the gazes of those around him.

“Even if I must devote this entire body, I will surely make you happy. No one can stop my heart from going to you.”

I watched that scene with a startled heart.

*What the? Did you love your wife this much?*

Chapter 122: Don't You Know Why I'm Angry?
------------------------------------------
I had thought the woman the Ghost Spirit married was a villainess.

But the way she looked now was far removed from a villainess.

“I heard you’re having a hard time researching the Ghost Gate’s secret arts. Please don’t overdo it.”

“Thank you for saying that.”

The Ghost Spirit grinned from ear to ear at Im Hyang’s concern.

“Aren’t you hungry?”

“I’m hungry.”

“Let us eat.”

The Ghost Spirit, Im Hyang, and the martial artists who had followed her entered the inn where we were.

They sat down at a spot near where we were seated.

So their conversation was heard perfectly.

“To be honest, my parents opposed this marriage. They said the successors of the Ghost Gate have always been those who devote themselves entirely to research, so they would leave a woman lonely.”

“Ah, that is a misunderstanding. Nothing like that will ever happen. I promise. I will never do anything to leave you lonely.”

I hadn’t heard the details of why the Ghost Spirit’s married life had collapsed. When we last parted, the Ghost Spirit had said his married life was an even greater hell, so it seemed they had lived together until then.

If it had turned out that way because he neglected her to study the Regression Grand Technique? If that was the case?

‘Ghost Spirit, you won’t be able to keep this promise now.’

Of course, it might not be for that reason. Judging by the expression with which he looked at Im Hyang now, if she ran away, he would probably bet his entire fortune, even abandoning his family business. If he liked her this much, why had his married life become hell?

Ian sent me a telepathic message.

—So those two are the ones we’re trying to stop from marrying?

—That’s right.

—He likes her that much, how do we stop them? If we stop them now, we’ll become enemies, won’t we?

Not just any enemies. They would become sworn enemies.

We turned our ears once more to the couple’s conversation.

“My father might treat you coldly. Even if he does, please understand.”

“Of course. I know well how much the Sect Leader cherishes Lady Im.”

Could it be that the two of them separated because of their parents? Finding out that reason was the primary task.

They ordered their meal.

“I don’t like stir-fried dishes.”

The Ghost Spirit quickly spoke up at Im Hyang’s clear preference.

“That is fortunate. I dislike them as well.”

“Really?”

“I think we are a match made in heaven!”

But I saw it. The Ghost Spirit’s momentary hesitation. The reluctance of a man who actually liked them.

Poor Ghost Spirit. From now on, he would be eating all the stir-fried dishes. Indeed, right now, even if he had to live his whole life eating nothing but Wall Valley Pills, he would probably say yes.

When they began their meal, we started our conversation. We didn’t lower our voices to draw their attention.

“Do you want to get married?”

For people on the verge of marriage, the hottest topic would surely be marriage itself. Sure enough, I could feel the Ghost Spirit and Im Hyang pricking up their ears.

“I won’t.”

“Why?”

“Marriage is something accompanied by great responsibility, isn’t it? I don’t have the confidence to take responsibility for someone. Even taking care of myself is overwhelming.”

I couldn’t tell if that was really her thought, or if she was answering because she knew I was trying to stop the two of them from marrying.

This time, Ian asked me.

“Do you want to get married?”

“No.”

“Why?”

“I just want to live freely.”

“What about children? Don’t you want to have children?”

“Children…”

I had been asked this question exactly once before. By Seojin, the Ghost Spirit’s younger sister. The answer at the time had been obvious. Back then, it was a period when I was consumed by the guilt and rage of having survived alone; children, what children?

“I don’t have the confidence to raise them well.”

“Well. Not all parents raise their children well.”

Ian's expression darkened slightly. Setting aside the Ghost Spirit’s matter for now, she asked the question she had wanted to ask her.

“Do you resent your parents?”

“I resented them when I was young.”

When she was very young, she had been abandoned by her parents. Without knowing who her parents were, she had drifted all the way to the main sect.

“And now?”

“I don’t think much about it now. Hating them only hurts me.”

I had deliberately asked about her parents. Telling her not to bury the wounds in her heart.

Rather, dig up what was buried and speak of it, then regret it and kick the blankets thinking you spoke for nothing, and since you’ve already spoken, you might as well speak again. I was trying to make it into something trivial like that.

It is only after going through this process many times that heart wounds begin to fade slightly, but just bury them in your heart and forget? Not a chance. No matter how deeply you bury them, they don’t rot. They don’t disappear.

Ian looked at me and smiled.

“Our parents are probably eating well and living well somewhere, I suppose.”

It was then.

“That won’t be the case.”

The one who couldn’t hold back and cut in was Im Hyang.

“Whatever the circumstances, your parents must be heartbroken.”

She had clear food preferences, and she was the type to clearly state her opinions on matters like this.

Ian spoke to her gently.

“Not all parents love their children. Some think of their children merely as money-making tools because they gave birth to them, and some beat and abuse them, even selling their children, don’t they?”

Her last words weighed on my heart. Perhaps Ian had lived her whole life thinking her parents had sold her.

“That would be the story of a very small number of parents. Your parents must have had their own circumstances.”

Pity and compassion filled Im Hyang’s eyes as she looked at Ian regretfully.

Unlike her innocent appearance, one could tell she was someone who showed her emotions even to a stranger.

It could have been unpleasant if she thought it was intrusive, but Ian responded gently.

“Thank you for your consolation.”

“No, I meddled unnecessarily. I’m sorry.”

Im Hyang apologized, and Ian likewise spoke politely.

“No. You spoke with goodwill; I was the one who reacted too sensitively.”

And so the two apologized to each other.

Belatedly, Im Hyang introduced herself.
