“I am Im Hyang, the daughter of the Im Family Sword House.”

Im Hyang introduced the Ghost Spirit, and Ian introduced me.

“This is my martial brother.”

She usually introduced us this way to new people. Since we had learned the same martial arts, strictly speaking, it wasn’t wrong.

“I’ll treat you to drinks as an apology. Today seems to be a reunion for you two after a long time, so let us meet tomorrow or the day after, whenever you have time.”

At Ian’s suggestion, Im Hyang shook her head.

“No, if anyone buys drinks, it should be me.”

The Ghost Spirit, watching the two insist on buying drinks, stepped in.

“As it happens, I was planning to hold a modest celebration tomorrow with close friends, and I would like to invite you two as well.”

“We couldn’t possibly attend such a precious gathering.”

When Ian refused, Im Hyang stepped forward.

“No, please come. Only then will I feel at ease.”

With Im Hyang also insisting, Ian pretended to give in and accepted their invitation.

“Very well, we’ll visit tomorrow.”

Shortly after, the Ghost Spirit and Im Hyang, having finished their meal, bid farewell and left the inn first.

After they left, Ian puffed up with pride for no reason.

“Didn’t I do well?”

“Excellent. To the point where I think I could leave this to you and go.”

“Ah! You can’t do that. I still don’t understand why we have to stop their marriage!”

Ian watched them walking away outside the inn. Even as they walked, the Ghost Spirit couldn’t tear his eyes away from Im Hyang.

I recalled the Ghost Spirit’s final moments.

—Is it still hell?

—It is a hotter hell. I have no face to show, but I beg you. If he doesn’t listen to the end, tear off my lower garments.

—To that extent?

—More than that.

It wasn’t that I couldn’t stop them if I really tried, but I was worried that if I forcibly stopped them in this situation, he might end up even more unhappy. They say matters between men and women are the most difficult in the world.

“I still don’t really know what to do.”

* * *

The next afternoon, Ian and I visited the Ghost Gate.

As we followed the guiding martial artist into the inner courtyard, ghost energy could be felt everywhere.

—Ian, do you feel that energy on the left?

—Yes, it’s an energy I’ve never felt before in my life.

—It’s the Ghost Gate’s ghost energy.

It was a ghost energy with a different feeling from that of the Soul-Devouring Demonic Zone.

—Those who use this ghost energy must truly be dealt with cautiously. Because you never know what might spring out, the most important thing is not to panic. If you panic, you die.

—Yes, I’ll keep that in mind.

Entering the inner courtyard like that, we saw three figures talking in front of the flower garden.

The Ghost Spirit, Im Hyang, and the one person I had wanted so much to see—Seojin.

“Don’t trust my brother. Once he falls into work, he wouldn’t notice even if you sparred right beside him. My brother.”

Im Hyang laughed, taking Seojin’s words as a joke.

“I’m not joking.”

“Then I’ll have to put on a splendid sparring match. One grand enough to turn his head to me.”

As they were talking like that, they turned to look at us upon our entrance.

My eyes met Seojin’s.

Originally, I met her much later than now. That was why Seojin’s current appearance felt so unfamiliar.

So you too had such fresh, youthful days.

She had suited that rough life of a wanderer so well. She was smart, deep-thinking, and loyal.

That time couldn’t be associated with her current appearance. Because right now, she was a bright and cheerful girl who had grown up receiving much love in a prestigious family.

Ian’s telepathic message flew in.

—You didn’t look like that even when seeing the beautiful courtesans at Cheonhwaru. The way you look at that woman is quite extraordinary.

As expected, Ian knew me best.

—No jealousy allowed.

—Milady! It’s not jealousy, it’s curiosity!

Indeed, how could the way I looked at Seojin be ordinary?

My friend.

In this life, I won’t let you grow cold in a nameless field. You said you were satisfied with your life as you were dying, but I won’t let you meet such an end. That is a promise as a friend.

We went before them like that. The Ghost Spirit introduced us to Seojin.

Once the introductions were finished, Seojin showed interest in Ian.

“Your eyes are so beautiful; why do you wear a veil?”

I looked at Ian, sending her a glance to show her face. It’s the beauty trap, Ian!

She, having caught my meaning, removed her veil.

Exclamations of admiration spontaneously left the two women’s mouths, but unexpectedly, the target himself, the Ghost Spirit, showed no particular agitation.

I asked the Ghost Spirit.

“Young Master Seo, isn’t my martial sister pretty?”

I had been trying to show him that there were such beautiful women in the world.

“She is beautiful. Beautiful indeed, but our Lady Im is more beautiful.”

I could tell. That the Ghost Spirit was speaking from the heart.

They say love is blind, but at this point, it wasn’t mere bean pods in his eyes—it was as if he had been struck by a Soul-Devouring Technique.

I sent Ian a telepathic message.

—You ugly thing! Put your veil back on.

—I’ve already put it on. What is with that man!

—I was trying to stop the wedding with the beauty trap, but it seems impossible.

—He likes her that much, why stop them? They look good together.

It was then. Im Hyang looked at the Ghost Spirit with a stiffened face.

“What is the matter, Lady Im?”

“Do you not know why I am angry?”

“I do not.”

The Ghost Spirit made an expression showing he had no idea.

“Why could you not be honest? That lady is more beautiful, is she not? Did you think I would like it if you said that?”

She thought he was lying, since even she could see Ian was more beautiful.

“In my eyes, you are more beautiful.”

“That is a lie, is it not?”

“No. Truly, you are more beautiful. I could swear it upon the heavens.”

“Is it true?”

Only after confirming like that did Im Hyang’s expression relax.

At this moment, I was certain.

The way she had cut into our conversation yesterday, and the way she was now. She was someone who had to confirm things that could otherwise be let slide—a strong personality, if one could call it that.

If she had pressed the Ghost Spirit with that personality? Someday, the bean pods stuck in his eyelids would have disappeared.

Weary of her, he would have escaped from reality into researching the grand technique. And in doing so, their relationship would have worsened further.

This wasn’t solely her responsibility. She was showing without hiding what kind of person she was, but the Ghost Spirit himself was failing to see it.

Even now, the Ghost Spirit was smiling at Im Hyang, so pleased about something.

Sigh, Ghost Spirit. What are we going to do with this? Do I really have to grab and tear off your lower garments?

Chapter 123: Change of Plans.
-----------------------------
It's because neither of them is a villain.

If either Gwiryeongja or Im Hyang were a villain, their married life wouldn't have been hell for so long. Someone would have left, or someone would have killed the other.

It's because they are both ordinary people. A relationship and life of fighting and bickering until affection took root, of truly hating each other yet being unable to help but live together, yet absolutely never wanting to repeat that life.

"Now, please come inside. Food is prepared, so have a meal first."

Ian and I went inside. Seojin followed us.

"Let me guide you."

I was trying to connect Seojin's fate from my previous life with Ian in this one.

As we walked together, I sent a voice transmission to Ian.

—Ian, Seojin here is a candidate for second squad leader of Gwiyeongdae.

—Ah!

Perhaps because it was unexpected, her eyes widened and sparkled.

—Just imagine having a squad leader who can use Ghost Arts in the Gwiyeongdae you lead.

—That would be incredible. Besides, the character for 'ghost' is even in our unit's name!

—Whether to take this woman under your command is still up to you.

Because Ian had absolute faith in me, she didn't ask anything about 'why her?'

Instead, she spoke to Seojin.

"Congratulations on my brother's wedding."

"Thank you. I thought he'd never get married in his life, yet here he is."

Though it was their first meeting, Seojin answered cheerfully.

"Why? You have good looks and a good personality."

"Still, he's just a bookworm who only studied."

Seojin was feeling favorable toward Ian. I knew the reason well.

The Seojin of the past had also liked pretty women very much. She said seeing pretty things brightened her mood. She liked beauties, flowers, and pretty clothes. Those tastes ill-suited to a wanderer were likely due to having had a childhood smoothly raised in a prestigious family.

"I've only heard of it in stories; I've never experienced Ghost Arts before."

"It's nothing special."

Seojin stopped walking for a moment and raised her hand. Black smoke rose from her palm and instantly took the shape of a snake.

Though it was a small snake made of smoke, it was strangely lifelike, seeming as though it might transform into a pitch-black serpent at any moment.

Whoosh!

The snake suddenly lunged toward Ian. But before it touched her, it popped and scattered into smoke.

"So cool!"

"You weren't surprised?"

"Because there was no killing intent."

A strange light flashed in Seojin's eyes as she looked at Ian. No matter how little killing intent there was, anyone would flinch if a snake suddenly lunged at them. But Ian wasn't agitated in the slightest.

"Show me again next time."

"Next time, I'll bring out something bigger and scarier."

"Great!"

I thought their personalities would mesh well too. Both women had straightforward, unpretentious personalities and knew how to be considerate of others.

If only both Cheongmyeon and Seojin could enter Gwiyeongdae?

Cheongmyeon holding down the fort as first squad leader, Seojin brightening the mood as second squad leader. Already a pleasant picture.

But it wouldn't be easy. One was a Demon Lord candidate, and the other was blood kin of the Gwimun Lord.

Several guests had already arrived in the main hall, and more continued to arrive afterward. There were guests of Gwiryeongja and guests of Im Hyang.

Once all invited guests had arrived, Gwiryeongja and Im Hyang also entered the main hall.

Together we raised our glasses and celebrated their upcoming wedding.

Most of the invited guests were successors of prestigious families, so they were well-mannered.

But there was exactly one exception. He was Ju Myeong of Seojongpa, who had been Im Hyang's friend since childhood. He entered with a dark expression, very dissatisfied with this marriage.

Was it because of the alcohol he'd downed from the start? Or had he harbored feelings for Im Hyang all along?

Right there in front of Gwiryeongja, he spoke openly to Im Hyang.

"Honestly, as an old friend, I can't congratulate you on this wedding."

"Why not?"

"By nature, martial families should form ties with martial families to avoid future trouble."

Naturally, Gwiryeongja felt bad hearing that. Moreover, saying such things right next to him was nothing short of openly disrespecting him.

Since Ju Myeong was Im Hyang's guest, Gwiryeongja spoke to him in a pleasant tone.

"Our sect is also a proper martial faction dwelling in the murim."

"No one here thinks of Gwimun as a martial faction."

"How can you say that? Our sect has contributed greatly to the murim until now."

"Young Master Seo, do you think Gwimun's techniques are legitimate martial arts?"

For a moment, Gwiryeongja couldn't answer. No one had ever asked him in such a way, so he didn't know how to respond.

"Martial arts and sorcery are clearly different things."

As Ju Myeong crossed the line, a clear, ringing voice echoed through the main hall.

"Then shall we check how wonderfully you handle sorcery with your martial arts?"

The one who stepped forward was Seojin. Seeing her stare at her opponent with one eyebrow slightly raised, memories of old days came back to me. Even during her wandering days, one eyebrow would rise like that when she was angry.

When she extended her hand, black mist rose around Ju Myeong.

"Huh!"

Startled, Ju Myeong looked around and drew his sword.

"What? What is this?"

He began swinging his sword at the air. He was definitely seeing some kind of illusion.

And to my eyes, I could see what he was seeing. A pitch-black shadow with only eyes and a mouth was rushing at him. It looked so horrifying and real that, even knowing it was an illusion, my heart turned cold.

And to my eyes, I could also see the method to dispel it. It was the result of combining the Blood Eye Spiritual Water and the Divine Eye Technique.

Ju Myeong was swinging his sword here and there, creating a situation where people nearby could get hurt.

When Seojin withdrew her technique, Ju Myeong, who had been swinging at the air, soon realized he had fallen for the Ghost Arts. Even knowing it was an illusion, he had been swinging his sword without realizing it. That is the Ghost Arts of Gwimun—to affect someone even when they know.

He glared at Seojin.

"To humiliate me with such cheap sorcery, I'll never forgive—"

Slap!

His cheek turned. The one who strode over and slapped him was Im Hyang.

"Do you want to die? Haven't you sobered up? Who do you think you are to say you won't forgive?"

"You!"

"Are we really friends? Friends don't act like this. You're my only friend, yet you make me this miserable?"

At those words, Ju Myeong couldn't say anything. The words 'you're my only friend' seemed to strike him like a dagger.

Ju Myeong let out a short sigh and said,

"I'm sorry."

The rage that had flared like flames was already gone.

"I'm sorry for hitting you too."

"It's fine. You hit me several times when we were young, didn't you?"

Ju Myeong also apologized to Gwiryeongja and Seojin.

"I apologize. It was rude of me to belittle your family. I am sorry."

But he had to get the last word.

"Still, I hate this marriage! I oppose it!"

With that, Ju Myeong left the place.

My gaze turned to Gwiryeongja.

Gwiryeongja, it should have been you shouting like that.

'I hate this marriage! Don't congratulate me! Am I truly the only one who opposes this wedding, even the groom?'

Gwiryeongja tried to scold Seojin for stepping forward recklessly, but Im Hyang stopped him.

And she apologized to Gwiryeongja.

"I'm sorry. We've been friends since childhood, so he seems to have been quite upset about me getting married."

"Isn't it because he cherishes you so much, Lady Im?"

"If he cherished me, he wouldn't act like that. He just got drunk and made a scene. Forget it."

Watching Gwiryeongja's gaze toward her grow even deeper, I inwardly sighed.

'He's falling deeper.'

Seeing her today, Im Hyang was quite a good woman too. I thought that if Gwiryeongja could properly understand her personality, the two of them might get along well.

* * *

Gwiryeongja stepped outside for a moment during the banquet to get some fresh air.

He hadn't shown it in front of Im Hyang, but somehow his mood kept sinking.

It was because of Ju Myeong earlier. When Ju Myeong expressed his dissatisfaction and stepped forward, Gwiryeongja kept regretting that he should have stepped up instead.

Could Im Hyang see him as a coward?

He would have won if he'd stepped up. Should he have displayed his skills properly? No. His father had always warned against letting Gwimun get swept up in murim conflicts.

What murim conflict? It was just a quarrel between friends. He should have stepped up!

Gwiryeongja's mind wavered back and forth, unable to settle. He hated how he looked now, regretting too late, even more than not having stepped up.

Lost in thought as he walked, he arrived at his study.

He stood by the window and looked inside. When he was here, his heart was most at ease. Come to think of it, whenever he was scolded by his father or had a problem, he always came here. This place had been Gwiryeongja's sole haven and refuge.

Gwiryeongja knew.

He wasn't someone who would be happy after subduing Ju Myeong and making him kneel while cackling with glee. He was someone happiest when reading books, researching, investigating, and being quiet.

'By the way, I should show this place to Lady Im.'

He wanted to boast to Im Hyang about how much he read. Today, he felt that way particularly strongly.

Then someone spoke.

"You truly have many books."

Startled, Gwiryeongja turned around.

Behind him stood I, who had followed him out.

"I was feeling stuffy and came out for some air, and while walking I ended up here."

"I see."

"I think you're the first murim person I've met with so many books."

"I've loved reading since I was young."

"I see. There is someone around me who also likes books."

"Oh, really? Please introduce me if there's a chance."

"I shall."

I imagined Gwiryeongja's horrified expression when he learned that the other party was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. The displeased face of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon looking at Gwiryeongja came to mind, and a laugh escaped me.

Together with him, I naturally moved my steps toward the main hall.

"Young Master Seo and Lady Im, the more I see you two, the better you suit each other."

"If this is consolation because of what happened earlier, it's fine. I've forgotten it all."

"In my eyes, it looked like jealousy. Yet I understand that young master. If a friend I'd long liked were getting married, I too would want to go and overturn the wedding feast."

I eased Gwiryeongja's mood. Whether I stopped his marriage or not, it was only possible if I became somewhat close to him anyway.

And the fastest way to get close to someone is to tell them what they want to hear.

"Someday that young master will jump up from his sleep and drink cold water, thinking, 'Why did I do such a stupid thing back then?'"

Gwiryeongja laughed at that. Since that incident had ruined his mood, it was fitting to use it to clear his mood as well.

"You held yourself well. If you hadn't, it would be you jumping up from your sleep, Young Master Seo."

Gwiryeongja stopped his steps, looked at me, and suddenly asked,

"Have we perhaps met before?"

Gwiryeongja, do you feel some kind of fate? Well, if it's you, you might be able to feel something.

"This is our first meeting."

"But looking at you, you don't feel like a stranger."

"Are there not people who feel comfortable even at first meeting, like someone you've known for a long time? Like a friend. Someone you trust for no reason. Someone you just want to look after."

"It's not quite to that extent."

"Perhaps it's because I felt favorable toward you from the start, Young Master Seo. Seeing the books earlier made me respect you even more."

I continued to say pleasing things, dismantling his wariness. Even the worn-down Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Supreme Evil Lesser Demon couldn't withstand my Divine Art of Flattery; getting close to this young Gwiryeongja was nothing.

And the most important point of this Divine Art of Flattery is knowing when to stop and retreat decisively. Because I had to give him time to chew over my words and feel good.

"Well then, I'll go in first."

I left him and entered the main hall.

* * *

On the way back after the banquet, I asked Ian,

"What did you think of Seojin?"

Ian had been talking with Seojin throughout the banquet.

"I liked her. She's excellent material for a squad leader."

"My recommendations never fail."

"But there's one problem."

"It's whether she'll join our main sect, right?"

"Yes."

"Gwimun is originally a sect that stands between orthodox and heretical. Among Gwimun's past ancestors, there were those who belonged to our main sect. If we persuade her well, there will be no problem."

"You know a lot about Gwimun?"

"I studied a bit before coming."

Actually, it was all things I'd heard from Seojin in the past. During her wandering days, Seojin hadn't wanted to be tied down by factions. She hadn't wanted to belong anywhere, and because of that, she was someone who could go anywhere.

"Anyway, we'll have to return to the main sect soon, so we can't take her with us. We just need to make it so she can find us later."

When she eventually runs away from home feeling suffocated, we just need to make it so she finds Ian instead of living as a wanderer.

"You know, right? To contact us, you can leave your name at the Seoho Inns located throughout the Central Plains."

"I'll be sure to tell her when we part ways later."

Separate from Ian, I planned to keep an eye on Seojin's movements myself. So that the great branch of fate could connect to Ian.

"By the way, young master? What happened with stopping the marriage?"

"Change of plans."

It was a decision made after seeing Gwiryeongja and Im Hyang at today's banquet.

Chief Gwak of our main sect's Iron Room always says: Small differences make a masterpiece.

I believe that applies to human relationships too. Small differences will change the results. Even if you can't change the person, you can change the relationship.

We devote our lives out of loyalty moved by a single word, part ways feeling hurt by a single word, hate for a lifetime because of a single word. That's who we are.

So my new strategy was this:

"I won't stop Gwiryeongja's marriage, but I'll make it so the two can live happily."

It can't be helped, Gwiryeongja. You must marry. But instead of the gates of hell, I'll open a different door for you.

Chapter 124: Beyond the Door You Opened.
----------------------------------------
Gwiryeongja was busy tidying his study.

He took out each book one by one and dusted it off, moved them from here to there and back again, expending his heart and soul on an arrangement no one else would ever care about.

After organizing the books like that, he placed winter daphne here and there around the study, and by the time he had boiled water for tea, Im Hyang arrived.

“Young Master Seo.”

“Miss Im. Welcome. I have not cleaned up, so it is a mess.”

Anyone could see it had just been cleaned until it gleamed, but Im Hyang smiled and pretended not to notice.

“You have so many books.”

“I used to have more, but I cleared many of them away.”

“Even just the books here are truly a lot. I didn’t know you were someone who read this much.”

Gwiryeongja was in an excellent mood.

“This book here was written by the author himself. I received it from him in this very room. And this book here is one I obtained after going all the way to Xizang. If you ever wish to know about ghosts, you can read this book. Shall I lend it to you?”

“Ah, ah. Yes.”

Gwiryeongja took out the book and handed it to her.

“It will be very interesting. Ah, if you find this interesting, try reading this one as well.”

“That one is all right.”

“This book here is……”

Gwiryeongja explained the books. Im Hyang could not bring herself to stop him when he was so excited, so she simply listened. But when the explanation grew too long, she said,

“Shall we drink tea?”

“Ah, I will brew it for you.”

Gwiryeongja was filled with heat, like the boiling water.

He could not forget the day he first saw her.

Gwiryeongja had attended a banquet of young talents held somewhere, and that day, too, he had been on the outside looking in.

Even if they had not deliberately ostracized him, he felt that they were subtly looking down on him. Their prejudice against the Ghost Sect was one thing, but fundamentally, Gwiryeongja was not the sort of person who mingled well with others. What did they all have so much to say? What did they all have so much to boast about?

When he stepped outside because he felt stifled, Im Hyang was there. He thought she was an exceedingly beautiful woman with a certain air about her, and then she spoke to him first.

“Isn’t it rather dull?”

Gwiryeongja was flustered. She was the first woman to speak to him at that day’s banquet.

“The banquet, I mean. The musicians today, the taste of the wine, and the people gathered here as well.”

It was something Gwiryeongja could never have imagined doing. Speaking so casually to someone she was meeting for the first time.

“I am Im Hyang of the Im Clan Sword Family.”

“I am Seo Gong of the Ghost Sect.”

“Ah!”

In that instant, Gwiryeongja regretted it. He should not have revealed his sect. Or if he did, he should have revealed it only after they had spoken a little longer.

But her reaction was unexpected. Instead, she showed interest.

“I have heard a great deal about the Ghost Sect.”

“What have you heard?”

“That it is an exceedingly mysterious sect.”

He wanted to talk with her a little more, but someone called Im Hyang from inside.

“They are calling me. Then I will see you later.”

Im Hyang went inside first.

After waiting a short while, Gwiryeongja also went inside.

In the distance, he saw Im Hyang surrounded by several martial artists, talking with them. She was popular.

Im Hyang recognized him and waved. Because of that, the gazes of those beside her gathered on him, but she paid them no mind and smiled.

Even after Gwiryeongja returned that day, he kept thinking of her for a long while. For a time, he diligently attended banquets.

Bringing over the tea he had brewed with care, Gwiryeongja said with a smile,

“If there are any books you wish to read, borrow them all.”

* * *

Ian, who had been looking at the night sky by the window, said to me,

“Young Master? Do you know what wish people most often make when a shooting star falls?”

“What is it?”

“Ah! Uh! Damn it! Apparently it’s that.”

At her words, I laughed and said,

“My eyes and mouth are quick, so I will never miss one.”

“Will you really not?”

“Just watch.”

The two of us looked up at the night sky, fully prepared to make a wish without fail. Then Ian abruptly said,

“That was the first time I ever brought up my parents.”

When she had spoken with Im Hyang at the inn, she had brought up her parents.

“I had wanted to tell you about them at least once, Young Master. It seemed like the right time, so I said it.”

“I thought that might be the case.”

“Now then, please comfort me.”

“What comfort? To tell the truth, I do not know much about affection between parents and children either. You know, don’t you? My mother died when I was young, and my father has the most brusque personality in the world. So I do not really know what I should say to you.”

“To tell the truth, I am the same. In the past, when you were struggling because of the Cult Leader, I wanted to comfort you. But how can one comfort someone when one knows nothing? Rather, everything I should have learned from parents, I learned from you, Young Master.”

“We grew up with runny noses together. What could you have learned from me?”

“Even so, you wiped my nose for me, Young Master. I learned a lot from you and relied on you a great deal.”

I started to say something to comfort her, then stopped. I simply stood with her and looked up at the night sky.

“Oh, right. How is the operation going?”

“I am thinking about it.”

“It needs some kind of stimulus!”

“What kind of stimulus?”

“What about kidnapping Miss Im and then having Young Master Seo save her?”

“If we do something clumsy, there will be an accident.”

“There has to be something thrilling between a man and a woman!”

“Ian, think of the most thrilling moment in your life. Was there kidnapping, rescue, or anything like that in it?”

“……There was not.”

“No one else would have had that either.”

The thrills of my life came from the everyday. And most of them came from words someone had said to me.

Words my father said, words Ian said, words Seo Daeryong said, words Jangho said, words the Demon Venerables said, words Cult Leader Pungcheon or Gowol said, and words I said……

Those countless words piled up and piled up, and my life was changing. The person I was did not change easily, but my life was changing.

“That is why I keep trying to tell him. That person.”

At that moment, a shooting star streaked across the night sky.

Ah! Please…… Damn it!

* * *

A few days later, I rented an elegant manor and invited Gwiryeongja and Seojin. It was to repay them for inviting us to the banquet last time.

Gwiryeongja’s favorable impression of me had already risen, and Seojin also felt goodwill toward Ian. So the two of them readily accepted the invitation.

It was a modest banquet with delicious dishes prepared by a skilled cook and only the four of us present, but Gwiryeongja liked this sort of atmosphere more.

While Ian and Seojin were talking, I conversed with Gwiryeongja.

“Thank you for inviting us.”

“Not at all.”

We talked about this and that, and then I subtly broached the subject of marriage.

“Are the wedding preparations going well?”

“Thanks to your concern, they are going well.”

“Sometimes I envy married couples.”

“What do you envy?”

“They know each other so well, do they not? Just by looking into each other’s eyes, they think, ah, that person must be thinking this. Ah, in a situation like this, that person would judge and act this way. I think it would be wonderful to have at least one such person while living in this world.”

I had to pull Gwiryeongja down to earth. Right now, he was floating up and flying through the sky. I had to make him set both feet on the ground and calmly look at the woman before him.

“When you look at Miss Im, do you have a sense of what she is thinking?”

Gwiryeongja seemed to ponder for a moment, then shook his head.

“To be honest, I still do not really know.”

I said nothing. I let him think about that part for a while.

I did not rush.

I left the marriage talk there and earned his goodwill by talking about ghost arts. Since I had gained a deep understanding of secret arts in this field while learning the Space-Time Transference Art, the conversation about ghost arts flowed well. And with the Divine Art of Flattery added on top of that, Gwiryeongja became completely absorbed in speaking with me.

Even after that day, I tried to meet him by any means possible.

Gwiryeongja was busy, but there was no need to meet for a long time. A brief moment to share a cup of tea was enough.

On some days, I even waited for him in the reception hall of his house while circulating the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art.

When I met Gwiryeongja, we would talk about this and that, and then I would again bring up something related to marriage or women.

At first, when I kept going to see him, suspicion was plain on Gwiryeongja’s face—What is with this fellow, after one meal? What is he aiming for?—but as time passed, he secretly began to enjoy conversing with me. After all, he had never had a single friend in his life.

I had started intentionally, but I too gradually came to enjoy speaking with him. The Divine Art of Flattery became less and less necessary.

“My junior martial sister said that you must not treat a woman too well.”

“Why is that?”

“Because if you treat someone well ten times and then fail to do so once, it is human nature for them to feel disappointed.”

“Then would it not be enough to treat her well all ten times?”

“Would a person’s heart not change as the years pass?”

“Mine will not change.”

“Young Master Seo is human too, so you will change.”

“I am different! I will absolutely not change!”

When he was stubborn like this, I wanted to rush at him and shout,

You did change! You really do change! Ugh, forget it, forget it. Let us just pry it loose, pry it loose.

“Do not treat her too well. If you go to the marketplace, bad men are popular.”

“Ah! I have felt that too. At banquets, I often wondered why anyone would like that sort of fellow.”

“So do not treat her too well. If you are a little lacking and then treat her well once in a while, would that not be moving? A person cannot always do well every time.”

“That makes sense.”

In his old age, Gwiryeongja had so desperately wished to converse with me. Back then, I had drunk only a single cup of wine and left again to gather ingredients.

The conversations we could not have then—let us truly have our fill of them now.

Time continued to pass, but I met him and talked with him as often as a mouse slips in and out of a basket of glue.

“You are saying that the date you must go obtain an important ingredient needed for your ghost arts training overlaps with Miss Im’s birthday?”

“That is right. Then which would you choose?”

“Of course I would attend to Miss Im’s birthday.”

“Even though it is an important ingredient? If I miss it this time, I must wait three years.”

“Even so, it is not even worth comparing.”

Someday, when something similar happens, Gwiryeongja, make sure you remember what you said today! There must surely be a difference between words never spoken and words uttered at least once.

I tried.

I spoke as though I did not know anything, persuaded him, and urged him on. I gave him courage, gave him hints, and gave him information.

Gwiryeongja had betrayed me, who had gathered the materials for the Great Art my whole life, and had tried to go back to the past himself, but even so, I was grateful that I had come here thanks to him, and I tried until the very end.

At last, one day before the wedding, I met him for the final time.

“To think I would meet you even today.”

Beginning like that, Gwiryeongja revealed his honest feelings.

“To be honest, I still do not understand. Why you are treating me so well.”

It was a question he had endured and endured, and it was also proof that we had become that close.

“Did you not say you liked Miss Im from the moment you first saw her? I was the same. When I looked at Young Master Seo, for some reason you felt like an older brother, like a friend, and I felt fondness toward you. I have no other intentions. The reason I saw you so excessively often is that once the wedding ends tomorrow, I intend to leave again as well, and I do not know when I will see you next.”

Gwiryeongja’s gaze was still suspicious.

I raised my hand high toward the sky.

“I swear to the heavens. If my words are false, I will be dragged away by the Heavenly Demon!”

Gwiryeongja waved his hands in alarm.

“Stop! There is no need to make such a dreadful oath! I believe you. If it is an oath like that, I must believe it.”

He held out his hand.

“Thank you.”

“Congratulations on your marriage.”

We clasped hands. Come to think of it, including before and after my regression, it was the first time I had ever held his hand.

The next day, the wedding was held.

Ian was excited, saying it was her first time watching a wedding. Befitting the wedding of a prestigious family, it was splendid and full of sights to see. Ian carefully took in each and every thing, and when there was something she did not know, she even asked the people conducting the ceremony.

Of course, since she must not become the protagonist of the day, she wore a bamboo hat with a veil attached and clothes that did not reveal her figure.

As the wedding was nearing its end, I said to Ian,

“Now let us return to the cult as well.”

“Yes, Young Master.”

“Did you enjoy this trip?”

“I will never forget it for as long as I live.”

Since she had even left records in her book as we traveled, I could tell just how much she had enjoyed it.

Ian and Seojin shared a reluctant farewell. The two seemed to have become quite close. When I met Gwiryeongja, Ian spent a great deal of time with Seojin. I had left Seojin’s fate entirely in Ian’s hands.

My eyes met Gwiryeongja’s as he was exchanging greetings with guests in the distance. I waved to him. He too smiled and waved back.

I do not know whether my bond with him will end here, or whether we will meet again. I also do not know what sort of life he will live.

I only hope that the conversations we shared will bring changes to his life, and that those changes will gather together and alter the result.

I do not know what lies beyond the door you opened today. Whether there is only the regret everyone speaks of, or whether there is a companion who knows you just by looking into your eyes. But I will ask you for just one thing. No matter what hardships come, do not give up. Just as you waited for me your whole life, do not give up on your own life until the end. Beyond the door you opened for me, I too am moving forward like this without giving up, so I hope you will do the same. Gwiryeongja, please be happy.

Together with Ian, I left the Ghost Sect.

“Now, let us go back, Ian!”

Chapter 125: Wow, What a Man.
-----------------------------
Seo Daeryong was watering the flower in the pot placed in the Hwangcheon Pavilion Master's office.

“Second, just when in the world is the Pavilion Master going to return?”

He was the one who called the potted flower left behind by Ilhwa Geumjon “Second.” The nickname had come from a joke that he was the third most important thing in the office.

“He should have taken me with him. Just how much fun is he having without me that there’s no word from him at all?”

Without Geom Mugik and Ian around, it truly felt as though the pleasures of life had been cut in half.

He had stopped the regular drinking get-togethers with Jang Ho since last month as well. They had postponed them until Ian returned. Honestly, it was only fun when there were three of them. It only felt complete when there were three.

“Hmph! From now on, you be the First. From today, I’m the Second. Let’s handle everything together, we abandoned ones!”

In fact, he was already handling it all. Seo Daeryong had taken charge of and was managing most of Hwangcheon Gak’s major affairs. Although there were seniors, since everyone knew that Geom Mugik favored Seo Daeryong the most, he had naturally become the acting Pavilion Master. And Seo Daeryong handled that work better than anyone.

The period of Geom Mugik’s absence was the period he had grown, and the period he had been genuinely recognized.

Furthermore, he hadn’t missed a single day of learning blade techniques from Hyeolcheon Domma either.

Hyeolcheon Domma never spoke about Geom Mugik. It was as if he thought that if he mentioned Geom Mugik, he would be caught missing him.

Even when Seo Daeryong talked about Geom Mugik, he would pretend not to hear and say nothing.

‘Master, that in itself is what’s stranger.’

While he was standing by the window suppressing his longing for Geom Mugik and Ian, an Enforcer came in through the door.

“We’re ready.”

“Let’s go.”

Lazy Pavilion Master who knows where and what you’re doing, I’m off to work again.

Outside the building, as many as ten Enforcers were waiting. Usually, two Enforcers moved for every investigator, so just seeing the number ten made it clear that today’s operation was both extremely important and dangerous.

“Have you all equipped your White Tiger Armor?”

“Yes, we have.”

The White Tiger Armor was standard-issue protective gear distributed to the Enforcers. It could not be compared to genuine protective garments, but it still had the effect of blocking ordinary blades.

“Let’s go. There may be a clash, so you must stay on alert.”

The place they arrived at with the Enforcers was Daechwirim, where Daechwima resided.

Daechwima.

He was one of the Eight Demon Lords, and because he was always intoxicated, he was called the Drunken Demon. There were many rumors regarding him.

Some said the reason he was always drunk was because he had suffered a great wound to his heart, and there was also a rumor that his unique martial art utilized drunkenness. In any case, one certain fact was that the drunker he became, the stronger he grew.

The martial artists who followed him were called Drunken Guests, and they too were heavy drinkers who loved liquor.

The gate of Daechwirim was firmly shut.

When an Enforcer knocked on the gate, a Drunken Guest guarding the entrance opened it and revealed himself.

“Who is it?”

“We’re from Hwangcheon Gak. Open the gate.”

“What brings you here?”

Seo Daeryong raised the document he had brought and showed it.

“We’ve come to arrest the Drunken Guest, Do Ho.”

This fellow named Do Ho had driven a carriage while drunk and charged into a downtown street, killing two people and injuring more than ten. Afterwards, he abandoned the carriage and fled by himself.

The Drunken Guest answered without hesitation.

“Do Ho isn’t here. Go back.”

“How do you know? Whether Do Ho is in that vast Daechwirim or not.”

“If he’s not here, assume he’s not and go back.”

The Drunken Guest immediately shut the gate and went inside.

He had been somewhat prepared for this to happen anyway. It seemed that the subordinates of the Demon Lords felt they would seem weaker than other Demon Lords if they obeyed obediently. Since one didn’t listen, none of them listened.

Especially as Geom Mugik’s absence had grown longer lately, the authority of the Hwangcheon Gak martial artists had begun to lose its bite.

“Warn them, then break down the gate.”

“Yes.”

An Enforcer stepped forward and infused his voice with internal energy as a warning.

“If you do not open before I finish counting to ten, we will break down the gate and enter. One, two, three, four…… nine, ten.”

At the very moment they were about to smash the gate.

Creeeak.

The gate opened and someone revealed himself. It was a different Drunken Guest from the one who had come out earlier. He was Ho Yang, the person in charge of the Drunken Guests guarding the entrance.

Ho Yang glared at Seo Daeryong and said.

“Do you think you’ll be safe after invading this forest?”

“The Drunken Guest named Do Ho has committed a crime. This isn’t something to be rashly covered up. If you hand over Do Ho, we will leave.”

“Do Ho isn’t here.”

“Do not underestimate this pavilion’s information network. We have reliable information that Do Ho returned here. If it is revealed that he is here, you too will be guilty of harboring a criminal. Is that acceptable?”

In that instant, Ho Yang emitted a cold demonic energy.

As befitting the person in charge of the Drunken Guests on duty today, his aura was sharp. He had been confident that he could make a mere Hwangcheon Gak investigator back away, but Seo Daeryong did not retreat.

Instead, Seo Daeryong’s aura overwhelmed Ho Yang. It was the result of learning blade techniques from Hyeolcheon Domma without missing a single day and training diligently.

Just as Ho Yang unconsciously stepped back, a fierce-looking middle-aged man walked out from Daechwirim.

Slap!

The man ruthlessly struck Ho Yang’s cheek.

“You useless bastard! Getting pushed around by some investigator like that.”

Ho Yang, who had fallen over, jumped up and bowed his head.

“My apologies!”

The man who had come out was none other than Gu Mayeong, one of the Three Great Drunken Guests of Daechwirim and infamous for his foul temper. His notoriety was widely known. He was said to have a hot-blooded personality, his hands moving before he thought of the consequences.

Having identified who his opponent was, Seo Daeryong’s expression hardened.

‘Of all people.’

Giving off a strong reek of alcohol, Gu Mayeong walked out to stand before Seo Daeryong.

“Why the hell are you kicking up a fuss and ruining the taste of my drink?”

“The one called Do Ho from this place caused an accident while driving a carriage drunk.”

“It’s only natural for a Drunken Guest to be drunk, and accidents can happen when driving carriages. Why have you swarmed here in a group making such a damned fuss?”

“Even if one is a Drunken Guest, the law states that you cannot drive a carriage while intoxicated.”

“Bullshit. Do you follow every law?”

“At least I don’t kill people by driving a carriage while drinking.”

Gu Mayeong shoved his face right up to Seo Daeryong. Seo Daeryong frowned at the terrible stench of alcohol wafting off him.

“Look me straight in the eye.”

Seo Daeryong did not avoid his gaze.

“Go back and pin it on some suitable nobody. Look at my eyes. Got it?”

“That is impossible.”

“What did you say? Say it again.”

“I said it’s impossible. If you don’t step aside, I’ll arrest you too.”

Gu Mayeong’s expression twisted, and he pulled out the liquor bottle hanging at his waist.

He raised it high, trying to pour it over Seo Daeryong’s head.

The Enforcers standing behind simultaneously gripped their sword hilts.

They had the momentum to rush forward and fight to the death if the order was given.

Gu Mayeong’s gaze turned toward the Enforcers.

“Making a fuss. If you want to die, try drawing those swords.”

The Drunken Guests behind Gu Mayeong also took up stances to draw their weapons.

In the tense situation on the brink of conflict, Seo Daeryong raised his hand and stopped the Enforcers. A blade fight here was something that absolutely must not happen.

Just as I thought. Smirking, Gu Mayeong was just about to pour the liquor over Seo Daeryong’s head when—

—from somewhere came a low warning.

“If you pour that alcohol, I’ll cut off that wrist and fill that bottle with your blood to pour over your head.”

Having confirmed whose voice it was, Seo Daeryong’s expression brightened radiantly.

“Pavilion Master!”

When Seo Daeryong turned around, Geom Mugik was walking toward them.

“My people, you’ve had it rough.”

At his appearance, Seo Daeryong and the Enforcers welcomed him with great joy.

“Welcome, Pavilion Master!”

“Pavilion Master!”

“Welcome back.”

They welcomed Geom Mugik so warmly that one would think no other leader in the world had ever received such a reception.

After exchanging greetings with them, Geom Mugik walked toward Gu Mayeong.

“Did your Demon Lord teach you to pour alcohol over people’s heads?”

When Daechwima was mentioned, killing intent surged in Gu Mayeong’s eyes.

But Geom Mugik’s presence overwhelmed him. Without even emitting demonic energy, he constricted Gu Mayeong with his aura alone. The fine hairs all over Gu Mayeong’s body stood on end simultaneously with fear.

‘So the Second Young Master’s ki was at this level?’

Gu Mayeong felt the alcohol sober up instantly. He had only heard various rumors about the Second Young Master, but facing him directly gave off a completely different pressure.

“And to think you would try to pour alcohol over a Hwangcheon Gak investigator’s head. Did you just come out of some closed-door training? Haven’t you heard the rumors about me? Or did you think I wasn’t coming back just because I was away for a bit?”

Gu Mayeong could only glare at Geom Mugik fiercely, not daring to offer any excuse. When he had heard the rumor that Geom Mugik had slain the Demon Lord, he had thought it was ridiculous. He had assumed the rumors were inflated just because he was the successor, but now he realized it had been true.

“Go in and bring……”

Geom Mugik turned to Seo Daeryong and asked.

“Who was it again?”

“Do Ho.”

Geom Mugik spoke to Gu Mayeong again.

“You heard him? Bring the guy called Do Ho. Now!”

Gu Mayeong remained motionless, his expression twisted.

Without hesitation, Geom Mugik drew his Black Demon Sword. The sword qi radiating from the blade made the hearts of the Drunken Guests there shrink coldly.

“You’re a drunkard, but I’m a madman.”

When Geom Mugik took a step forward, Gu Mayeong could endure no longer.

“I’ll bring him out!”

Gu Mayeong went inside.

I had deliberately treated Gu Mayeong harshly. It was for the morale of Seo Daeryong and the Enforcers. Word of today’s events would spread throughout the sect, and in the future they wouldn’t dare commit such acts recklessly just because I was away.

Seo Daeryong and the Enforcers gathered around me.

“When did you return, sir?”

“Just now. I was going to change clothes and visit my father, but I heard you all came here, so I stopped by on my way to the Heavenly Demon Hall.”

Seo Daeryong was so moved that tears welled up.

“Is it that good?”

“I didn’t know I would be this happy to have the Pavilion Master return.”

“Isn’t it because you avoided being humiliated by that drunkard?”

“When I smelled it earlier, that bastard’s liquor was expensive stuff. When would someone on a meager salary like mine ever get to drink such fine alcohol? I should have at least tasted it flowing down my face.”

Geom Mugik smiled at Seo Daeryong. In the time they hadn’t seen each other, Seo Daeryong had grown even more. His martial arts and aura, of course, but also his composure. When dealing with someone like Gu Mayeong, one needed to be calm like this.

“I suppose I’ll have to leave the sect often?”

“Please don’t do that! I thought I would die of boredom without the Pavilion Master around.”

The Enforcers laughed at Seo Daeryong’s words. Geom Mugik felt good seeing them again. He hadn’t realized it when they were together, but after being apart and reuniting, he was overjoyed.

In the meantime, the gate opened again and Gu Mayeong dragged Do Ho out. His face was split open and blood was trickling down. It seemed Gu Mayeong had vented his anger on him.

“Arrest him!”

The Enforcers arrested Do Ho.

As if trying to display his last shred of pride, Gu Mayeong glared fiercely at Geom Mugik.

On the other hand, Geom Mugik spoke in a gentle tone unlike before.

“Since you’re Drunken Guests, I won’t tell you not to drink. But you should drink without causing harm to others. Thank you for your cooperation.”

With beast-like fellows like this, you couldn’t just push them into a corner. Because eventually they would inflict great harm on someone and self-destruct.

Since he had crushed his pride in front of his subordinates, it was also a matter of relationship management to cut him a little slack.

“Let’s have a drink next time. It’ll be my treat.”

At Geom Mugik’s words, Gu Mayeong merely snorted and went inside.

The Enforcers took Do Ho back to Hwangcheon Gak, and Geom Mugik and Seo Daeryong slowly began walking toward the Heavenly Demon Hall.

“Were you injured anywhere during this trip, sir?”

“No.”

“That’s a relief. I was very worried when I heard that Geugak Soma had returned alone.”

“Were you afraid he had killed me and come back alone?”

“I really wanted to go to Akingok and ask at that time.”

“Then there would have been a different investigator in your place today.”

“That’s exactly why I didn’t go. Now that the Pavilion Master has returned, I’ll take a few days of leave.”

“I’ll tell Elder Domma. That his beloved disciple is ready for hellish training, having even taken leave for it.”

“……Leave? What leave? I have to work.”

“Right? I finally came back, so there’s no way I can let you go anywhere.”

He smiled at Seo Daeryong. Joking around with him really made him feel like he had returned to the main sect.

“Huh!”

Suddenly Seo Daeryong stopped walking and whispered.

“Northwest. Pretend not to look.”

There stood a woman who caught the eye at a single glance.

“Was there such a beauty in the main sect? No, how can a person have such a figure? Ahh! She must have descended from the heavens.”

“Do you like her?”

“Who wouldn’t?”

“Want to meet her?”

“Oh my, I don’t think I could even make eye contact with her. Huh? What did you just say? Meet her? How?”

“She came with me this time.”

“What? Huh?”

Seo Daeryong looked at Geom Mugik in surprise. Then he soon shook his head.

“Come on, don’t lie.”

“It’s true. We’re close.”

“The Pavilion Master knows such a beauty? You’re close? Why would you put on such airs between us? Wow, now I see the Pavilion Master is a man too.”

Geom Mugik beckoned to the woman.

Then the woman began walking gracefully toward the two of them.

Chapter 126: I’ll Fight and Take the Credit
-------------------------------------------
As the woman approached, Seo Daeryong grew flustered.

“Huh? She’s coming this way?”

“See? I told you.”

“She’s coming because you beckoned her over. She’ll come and say this: Why did you call me? Right? Then you’ll have to answer like this: I called you to tease this friend here.”

The closer the woman came, the more Seo Daeryong’s eyes lost focus and the wider his mouth fell open. She wore a face veil, and it only made her seem all the more mysterious. To Seo Daeryong’s eyes, she was like a fairy descended from the heavens.

Of course, she was Ian. She came right up to the two of them and stopped.

Geom Mugeuk said to Ian,

“I told him I came with you, but he doesn’t believe me.”

At that, Ian smiled with her eyes and said to Seo Daeryong,

“You’re Investigator Seo, aren’t you? I’ve heard so much about you.”

Ian spoke in a disguised voice, hiding her original one.

Startled that the woman knew him, Seo Daeryong first sent a sound transmission to Geom Mugeuk.

—How does she know me?

—Because I told her.

—How? Why? You never once told me you knew such a beauty. With your personality, Pavilion Lord, there’s no way you wouldn’t have bragged about it! A strong heart and a strong right arm are all well and good, but what I have is the prettiest face!

While Seo Daeryong was busily sending me sound transmissions, Ian said with a smile,

“I hear you’re the Pavilion Lord’s right arm?”

At that moment, unconcealable joy surged across Seo Daeryong’s face. After all, was not the position of right arm an incredibly precious and magnificent seat coveted by many?

“Oh, really, why would he tell you even that?”

Though he acted bashful, Seo Daeryong’s shoulders were rising toward the heavens.

“He told me this too.”

“What did he say?”

“That you have a cold-hearted, power-oriented personality, yet you’re a true man who loves only one woman; that when everyone else keeps silent, you alone raise your hand, making you both a rebel and a pacifist; and that you’re someone who made a life-changing decision over a cup of wine at an inn?”

Seo Daeryong was stunned. The beauty before him had recited word for word the things he had once gone around saying as jokes. Seo Daeryong looked at Geom Mugeuk with an expression of utter disbelief.

“You introduced me in quite some detail.”

“And not only that, I heard you hold drinking gatherings regularly.”

“You even told her that?”

Seo Daeryong was flustered. This beauty knew far too much about him.

‘Why did the Pavilion Lord tell her about me in such detail?’

That did not mean he was displeased. How could it possibly feel bad that a beauty one might not see even once in a lifetime knew so much about him?

Ordinarily, he had been confident. If someone ever tried to use a beauty trap on him, he would never fall for it. What mattered was the heart; what did appearances matter? That was how he had lived. All the more so in a murim where people using the Red Face Art to deceive others about their age and suck the life force from their victims openly roamed about.

But seeing the woman before him, he thought that if a woman like this truly set her mind to seduction, it would not be easy to resist. She had nothing to do with him, yet he found himself pointlessly wanting to look good in her eyes.

“Do you not hold your drinking gatherings these days?”

“One person is missing, so we’ve put them off for a while.”

Only belatedly did Seo Daeryong think, Ah, and ask Geom Mugeuk,

“Ah! What about Martial Artist Yi?”

“You sure ask quickly.”

Geom Mugeuk’s expression immediately turned gloomy.

“You won’t be able to see her anymore.”

Seo Daeryong flinched in surprise.

“Why?”

“……”

“Don’t tell me. Nothing happened, right?”

“What if it did?”

“Why are you doing this? This isn’t funny. You can joke about anything else, even if someone gets hurt, but don’t joke about this. It won’t work on me.”

“It’s not a joke. You won’t be able to see that Ian anymore.”

Seo Daeryong stared at Geom Mugeuk, then laughed.

“Shall I tell you the decisive reason this sort of joke won’t work?”

“What is it?”

“Because of you, Pavilion Lord.”

“Me? Why me?”

“She went out with you, and something happened to Martial Artist Yi? That’s impossible.”

Then, all of a sudden, Seo Daeryong’s thoughts reached somewhere else.

“Ah, now that I think about it, Pavilion Lord. This is too much.”

“What is it now?”

The next words flew in by sound transmission.

—You met a beauty like that, and in the meantime you threw our Martial Artist Yi aside. As a fellow man, I understand, but still, you can’t do that to Martial Artist Yi. Do you know how much she thinks of you normally? Where is she? Don’t tell me you sent her to the training ground to train the moment you arrived. Ah, if you did, I’ll be disappointed in you, Pavilion Lord.

—Hey, let me talk too.

—Enough. Bring out Martial Artist Yi! No, I’ll go find her myself.

Seo Daeryong asked Ian with a smile,

“By any chance, have you seen a somewhat well-built female warrior?”

“Ah, I have.”

With an expression that said, I knew it, Seo Daeryong said to Geom Mugeuk,

—This kind of joke won’t work on me. I entered Hwangcheon Pavilion at the top of my class, you know. And from now on, I’m going to call you the Lecher Pavilion Lord.

—What did I do?

—You abandoned Martial Artist Yi and brought that beauty back instead! Ah, you really are wicked. Now even that innocent beauty suddenly looks hateful to me.

Seo Daeryong greeted Ian.

“Then I’ll see you next time.”

As he headed toward Ian’s quarters, Seo Daeryong sighed inwardly.

‘Ah, I didn’t even ask her name. Well, it’s not as if I’ll ever see her again anyway.’

Just then, Ian spoke from behind him.

“She isn’t at the training ground right now.”

“Pardon?”

How had this woman known he was about to go search the training ground first?

When Seo Daeryong was startled, Geom Mugeuk said something even more shocking.

“I passed along to her everything you’ve sent me by sound transmission until now.”

Seo Daeryong was startled.

—Why?

—Speak out loud, not by sound transmission.

Seo Daeryong said with an expression of complete incomprehension,

“No, why? Why would you send sound transmissions to this lady? No matter how beautiful she is, you weren’t this kind of person, Pavilion Lord. You’ve really become strange.”

At that, Ian said,

“I’ve become strange too, haven’t I?”

What kind of strange thing was that supposed to mean? Just as Seo Daeryong blinked, Ian took a dagger from within her robes.

It was the very dagger made from melting down Gowol’s ten-thousand-year cold iron shackles.

The moment he saw that dagger, Seo Daeryong was startled and demanded of Geom Mugeuk,

“Why does this lady have the dagger?”

Seo Daeryong, who had been looking at Geom Mugeuk with suspicious eyes, flinched.

“Don’t tell me you took Martial Artist Yi’s dagger and gave it to this young lady. That can’t be it, right? That one is yours, Pavilion Lord, right?”

“Mine is right here. That one really is Ian’s.”

When Geom Mugeuk took out his own dagger and showed it to him, Seo Daeryong shouted,

“You truly cannot do this! This is too much!”

For the first time, Seo Daeryong flared up at Geom Mugeuk like fire.

Geom Mugeuk merely smiled. When he only smiled, Seo Daeryong grew even angrier.

“What in the world happened while you were out? Have you gone mad for women? That dagger was shared among the people who were there that day, wasn’t it? Of course you were the one who gave them to us, Pavilion Lord, but still, isn’t that a dagger that holds great meaning for all of us? And yet you took Martial Artist Yi’s dagger and gave it to that woman? Whose will you give to the next beauty? Here, I’ll give you mine in advance, so take it. No, wait, you can’t take this. I’ll give my dagger to Martial Artist Yi.”

“I didn’t know you cared for Ian this much.”

“She’s a friend I can tell everything in my heart to. She’s my drinking friend.”

Just then, Ian said,

“Let’s go drink.”

Seo Daeryong flinched. The words of refusal did not come out immediately. Still, he gritted his teeth and forced out his rejection.

“Now is a bit difficult.”

Seo Daeryong sighed inwardly.

‘To think I refused when a beauty I may never see again in my life asked me to drink! Why did I have to meet her in a situation like this? Ah, is there any life as ill-fated as mine?’

But even so, Ian came first.

“Right now, I have to look after my abandoned drinking friend first. Then, if you’ll excuse me.”

“Thank you for thinking of me this much.”

Seo Daeryong looked at Ian as if asking what she meant.

Ian removed the face veil she had wrapped around herself. In that instant, it felt as though light were bursting from her face.

At the beautiful face revealed before him, Seo Daeryong’s eyes went wide, but he soon hardened his expression. Now was not the time to admire the beauty before his eyes.

“I still won’t go! I can’t go!”

“Didn’t we promise that when I returned, we’d drink ourselves senseless all night long?”

“Pardon?”

“It’s me. Ian.”

Thrown into a cauldron of confusion, Seo Daeryong only blinked.

“Didn’t we say that in autumn, the three of us would go with Palace Lord Jang to Daecheonsan to drink while looking at the autumn leaves, and in winter we’d drink while snow fell on us?”

When Ian spoke in her own original voice, Seo Daeryong jumped in surprise.

“The voice is right. Are you really Martial Artist Yi?”

“Yes, it’s me.”

“……No, you aren’t.”

“I’ll tell you everything about what happened over drinks, when Palace Lord Jang is there.”

Because the way she spoke was Ian’s, only then did Seo Daeryong realize that she truly was Ian.

“You really are Martial Artist Yi!”

For one final confirmation, Seo Daeryong looked at Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk nodded once, confirming it.

“What was that you said? Lecher Pavilion Lord? Mad for women?”

“Ahhh! I’m sorry!”

Geom Mugeuk smiled at him, who was still bewildered.

“No. You did well. You scored a mountain of points with Ian today.”

At that, Ian said loudly,

“Today’s drinks are on me to the very end.”

“Uwaaaah! Excellent.”

Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“May we go drinking? With Palace Lord Jang as well.”

How difficult the Divine Poison Purification Art had been, and how wonderful and fun the journey had been—her face openly showed that she was dying to talk about it all, making Geom Mugeuk smile on his own.

“Drink to your heart’s content today!”

Watching Ian and Seo Daeryong’s excited backs as they left, Geom Mugeuk slowly turned his steps toward the Heavenly Demon Hall.

* * *

The Heavenly Demon Hall was unchanged.

Beyond the Path of Blood, Father sat. I slowly walked over the red carpet to where Father was.

Seeing Father again made me so happy. Who would have thought that his blunt expression and gaze would feel this welcome?

“Father, have you been well in the meantime? I thought I would die without seeing you again.”

I gave Father a deep bow.

“You look as though you have returned from fighting a war.”

“I fought a great war.”

“That is what happens when you needlessly meddle in the affairs of the Evil Dao Alliance.”

“It was not needless. At the very least, I judged that Bi Sain would be easier for us to control than Seok Gwanchu’s grandson.”

Under normal circumstances, it would have been more accurate to say he was a better person, but I said easier to control. Father extremely disliked and guarded against hasty evaluations of people.

“It would have been more advantageous for us if Seok Gwanchu’s grandson became the successor.”

“Why is that?”

“Because that one is far greedier.”

“Wouldn’t the murim become even more chaotic instead?”

“What does that have to do with us?”

I know those words are sincere. After all, the more chaotic the murim became, the more powerfully our cult had exerted its strength.

Father. Even so, surely we cannot continue to grow by feeding on blood forever.

“Why did you not return at once? Were you not concerned about your elder brother taking control of the Demon Supremes?”

“I was concerned.”

“Then why?”

“I never intended to make all the Demon Supremes my people.”

“Why?”

“Because then my brother would be driven into a corner.”

“!”

“If that happens, my brother will ultimately make the worst choice, one that cannot be undone. A choice I would not be able to overlook. I told you, did I not, that the bloodshed in the struggle for succession would stop with my generation?”

Father could not hide his surprise.

“You would leave him a breathing space for that reason? Did you truly think that far?”

“I have to. When I think of how great and tremendous the things I gain by fighting are, I must think deeper and farther than that. I will fight while using my head, making sure to boast of it to you like this as well, Father.”

Father silently gazed at me. I also quietly looked back at Father.

“I would like to ask one thing as well.”

“What is it?”

“When Seok Gwanchu or his master Baek Manggi moved, were you worried about me even a little?”

“I was not.”

“Why not? Did you trust me that much?”

“I did not trust you. I trusted Geugak.”

“You trusted Geugak Soma?”

“Yes. If he was by your side, it must have been because he judged that it was certainly a fight worth taking. I trusted Geugak’s judgment.”

“From now on, please trust your son a little.”

“If I had used you as the standard at the time, our cult would have had to march out to rescue you.”

“Ah! When will I become a trusted son?”

At my exaggerated words, Father still wore an expression that said I was nowhere close. I thought that perhaps even if I became an immortal and ascended to the heavens, Father might look at me with that same expression. And as he watched me rise into the sky, he would say, Do not trust immortals!

“Go and rest.”

“Then I will take my leave.”

I turned to leave, and from behind me, Father spoke.

“You have returned well.”

The moment I heard those words, my chest tightened. It was a short phrase, but more than any other words, they were the ones I had wanted to hear from Father.

I turned back toward Father and silently bowed my head.

Thank you, Father, truly, for steadfastly holding your place there.

After leaving the Heavenly Demon Hall like that, I set off toward the place where the person I had wanted to see most after Father was.

Chapter 127: That Man Always Reeks of Alcohol.
----------------------------------------------
The next person I went to see was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

After my father, my feet had naturally carried me to him.

I had expected to find him reading by the window in his room, but he was in the courtyard, swinging the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber as he trained in his saber arts. When he noticed I had arrived, he drew back his blade.

“You’re here?”

He greeted me as casually as if we had seen each other yesterday. Because it was not emotional, I could feel the emotion in it—a reunion very much like the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“You look impressive when you read, but you look even more impressive when you wield a saber.”

“What’s with the flattery?”

“I survived thanks to you, Elder.”

I spread open the front of my clothes and showed him the Ghost Tiger Robe he had given me.

There were several slashes where it had been cut by a sword. They were none other than the marks left by the Sword Emperor, Baek Manggi.

“I survived thanks to you. Thank you, Elder.”

Of course, the supreme-grade Heavenly Silkworm Silk had played its part as well. Those two layered treasures had saved my life.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon came closer and looked at the marks left by the sword. What flashed through his eyes was unmistakably relief.

“You were worried about me, weren’t you?”

“Worried, my foot! I was checking to see whether you’d ruined a precious treasure garment. Fine, you can keep using it.”

I knew without needing to see it. He surely had not said a word about me to Seo Daeryong. Even though he had been this worried.

“So the rumor was true.”

“What rumor are you talking about?”

“The rumor that the Sword Emperor, Baek Manggi, is dead.”

His death was information that had not yet been officially revealed to the outside.

“Did you know about that man’s swordsmanship?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon shook his head.

“Then why do you think these marks are Baek Manggi’s?”

“If it weren’t Baek Manggi, then with you and Extreme Evil joining forces, no one could have left sword wounds like these.”

“How did you know I fought together with the Extreme Evil Soma?”

“Follow me.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon led me into the house.

Then he took something out of a wardrobe and tossed it to me.

To my surprise, it was a white mask worn by the Faceless Guests.

And it was not an ordinary mask, but the very top-grade one the Extreme Evil Soma wore. The one that was light, let even air pass through well, and especially made it impossible to see the eyes from the outside.

“Did you fight the Extreme Evil Soma and kill that man?”

“Even if I wanted to kill him, do you think that bastard would die so easily?”

“Then why is this coming out of your wardrobe, Elder?”

“Did the Extreme Evil Soma not say anything?”

“I haven’t met the Extreme Evil Soma yet since I returned. I stopped by the Heavenly Demon Hall, then came straight to you, Elder.”

“You didn’t go to the Valley of Villains first?”

“Of course I had to come to you first, Elder. Why did you think I went to the Extreme Evil Soma first?”

Then an unexpected answer came out.

“The Extreme Evil Soma came by.”

“To you, Elder? What did he say when he came?”

“He left that with me. Told me to give it to you when you came.”

“He left this with you, Elder? Why?”

“That is what I want to ask. Why did he leave this ill-omened thing with me?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked at me with suspicious eyes.

Since Ian had taken the one he had given me, it seemed he had made another. Once he returned to the cult and was making one anyway, he must have decided to make it top-grade since he was giving it to me.

“What in the world are you scheming?”

“You should have asked the Extreme Evil Soma that question at the time.”

“I did.”

“And?”

“He only told me to give it to you and left. What the hell is he trying to do?”

“He must have been afraid it would get lost if he entrusted it to just anyone.”

“Who in this cult would lose something that man entrusted to them? They’d clear away their family heirloom and put this mask in its place.”

I laughed loudly, but the Blood Heaven Blade Demon did not laugh. He had realized that something had happened between me and the Extreme Evil Soma.

“Well, since he went out with you, something must have happened. What exactly happened?”

“He promised to give me a mask.”

“Why?”

“It is a long story if I am to tell it. If you want, I will tell you everything.”

“Forget it. What would I do with such a trivial story?”

“The reason he entrusted the mask to you was to show off our friendship and drive a wedge between you and me, Elder.”

“So why?”

How could the quick-witted Blood Heaven Blade Demon not know that intent? What he wanted to know was the reason for that estrangement.

“I do not know either. How could I know what is in that man’s heart?”

After a brief pause, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon spoke.

“If you trust the Extreme Evil Soma, you will regret it later.”

Those words did not come from something like jealousy. It was a warning that traveling the road we had to take together with the Extreme Evil Soma was dangerous.

“I do not trust that man.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon stared at the mask in my hand with those small, narrow eyes of his.

“Is that so…”

He said no more, but I could guess what he had been about to say.

Will that really go as you intend? When the Extreme Evil Soma is reacting this actively?

He likely refrained from saying it because, if he did, it might look as though he was paying attention to the Extreme Evil Soma.

“Elder, why do you dislike the Extreme Evil Soma?”

“Go ask him. Ask him whether he likes me. It is the same for both of us. Why are there only hateful bastards in this cult?”

I laughed again. The words, “Isn’t that because of your personality, Elder?” rose to my throat, but I held them back and swallowed them down. As if he did not know his own temper when he said that.

“Now I suppose we should talk about my hateful bastard. Did my brother come see you again while I was gone?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon shook his head.

“After he came before, I did not give him an answer. The Eldest Young Master must have taken that as a clear refusal.”

“As expected, you are reliable.”

“Do not let your guard down. I am someone who could go to the Eldest Young Master at any time.”

“Then I would be going with you.”

“What do you mean?”

“If the wings attached to my body fly away, I will go with them, won’t I?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked dumbfounded.

“As long as you go around wearing such an ill-omened mask, those wings will fall off in no time.”

Though he said that, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was not someone who would leave so easily. Even when the Extreme Evil Soma tried to sow discord, he was accepting it with a broad heart. That was why the first person I had come to see was this Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Even if you go over to my brother, Elder, I will still come to see you. I will fling open the door to a room where a plan to get rid of me is in full swing and look for you, Elder.”

“Why?”

“Because I will want to see you. Because even then, I will come to want to see you.”

“More blatant flattery.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon snorted, but he could not hide that he was pleased. Now the door to his heart had opened quite a bit, and I could see him smiling inside it. Yes, that looks good. Please smile like that more often.

“Then I will come see you again. Let us have a drink at Pungnyu Tavern sometime soon.”

As I said farewell and rose from my seat, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked,

“Are you going to the Extreme Evil Soma?”

“Yes. Since I received a gift, I should offer my greetings.”

“You would have gone even if it were not a gift, wouldn’t you?”

“I probably would have.”

I did not bother lying to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Always honestly. No matter how uncomfortable or unappealing the matter was, always honestly. I believed this was the greatest strength that would keep me from losing the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

That was why the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, too, must be giving me this advice sincerely.

“I do not know what happened outside, but the Extreme Evil Soma is ultimately the Extreme Evil Soma. You must never forget that.”

“I will keep it in mind.”

After leaving the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s residence like that, I went straight to meet the Extreme Evil Soma.

I put on the mask the Extreme Evil Soma had given me and entered the Valley of Villains.

The gazes of the Faceless Guests focused on me. They all looked like the same white masks, but the Extreme Evil Soma’s mask stood out. Since I was wearing the mask their Demon Venerable wore, they were all surprised.

Under those startled gazes, I was guided all the way to the Extreme Evil Soma’s room.

His room was exactly the same as when I had come before.

Not only the walls on every side, but even the floor was painted entirely white, and there was still nothing in the room. No table, no display cabinet. In that empty space stood only the Extreme Evil Soma.

There was only one difference from then.

When I first came, he had been looking at the wall, but today he was looking out the window.

“I have come, Lord Soma.”

The Extreme Evil Soma turned around. When he saw me wearing the mask, the eyes within the eyeholes revealed delight.

“How is the mask?”

“It is good. Incomparably better than the previous one.”

“Then that is enough.”

“Why did you leave this with Elder Doma?”

“I heard the two of you were the closest.”

“And?”

“I did not like seeing the Second Young Master close to anyone besides me. I do not know for certain, but that narrow-minded old man must be quite bothered by it.”

I pushed the mask up onto my head and asked,

“Do you only feel satisfied if you are the closest?”

“That is right. That is my personality. From now on, you must be closest to me. If that cannot happen, I will go over to the Eldest Young Master.”

“I have received your selfishness, Lord Soma.”

The Extreme Evil Soma laughed loudly. It was hard to tell where the joke ended and where his sincerity began.

At least one thing was certain.

Making one of the Eight Demon Venerables my ally and making two of them my allies did not require twice the effort. It required three times, four times the effort. They were water and oil that would never mix.

“How was the trip with the prettier heart?”

“It was truly good.”

“As expected, traveling is best when one goes with a beauty, isn’t it?”

“That beauty liked it so much. It was such a late trip that I felt sorry.”

“Do not fall for a woman. In the end, she will betray the Second Young Master.”

“The Mistress of Cheonhwaru would be disappointed to hear that.”

“She knows as well. That I do not trust her. You cannot betray someone who does not trust you.”

“Then you must not trust me either, Lord Soma.”

“I do not trust humans. Being close is one thing, and trust is another matter.”

I was grateful that he openly told me, This is what I am like. Because the problem in relationships with people had always been the disappointment that came when I realized the other party had been wearing a different mask.

“Did my brother come by?”

The Extreme Evil Soma added other information to his answer.

“He came by twice. It seems he has been diligently meeting not only me, but also the Poison King and the Fist Demon.”

“For now, we have the advantage, then?”

“How does that calculation work?”

“Even if we say the Poison King and the Fist Demon have joined hands with my brother, adding the Demon Buddha makes three. On the other hand, we have you, Lord Soma, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, and the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. Four against three. We have the advantage.”

“It is not four against three.”

“Why not?”

“To begin with, I am neither on the Eldest Young Master’s side nor on the Second Young Master’s side.”

He had achieved his goal of making it so that he was neither on my side nor my brother’s. Of course, emotionally, my feelings toward him were more intimate than that.

“Then we are at a disadvantage. Our Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable is still too weak to count as one person.”

“Do you think it is only the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable? Are you certain the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable are on the Second Young Master’s side? Did you write a blood-sealed pledge? You must not ignore what the Eldest Young Master is offering. Money and power—very concise and clear. He is even clearly presenting the amounts and positions. He knows he is at a disadvantage, and he is putting forth the maximum he can.”

It was also a breathing hole I had allowed. Yes, Brother. You should do your best as well. There are words that pass between those who are both doing their utmost.

“When complex and troublesome problems arise, humans ultimately choose the simplest thing. At the very least, the Eldest Young Master feels simpler than the Second Young Master.”

Perhaps the Extreme Evil Soma was trying to show me.

How easily humans are shaken. How easily human relationships collapse.

That was probably why he had entrusted the mask to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. To show me that a relationship could crack for that simple reason alone.

Of course, it did not work because the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s heart was broader than he had expected.

He was advising me. That if I approached this emotionally, I would fail.

“I will make generous offers too. No matter what, twice as much as my brother! How about it? Are you tempted?”

“Do you have that much money?”

“I will have to earn it from now on. If you have money, please lend me some.”

At that, the Extreme Evil Soma suddenly launched himself backward and flew to the rear wall.

“That is too much.”

“Do not forget. The one borrowing money is always the one being too much.”

We looked at each other and laughed at the same time.
